## 3. Target State

**Context and tag legend for Sections 3 to 5**

Research question context: options analysis and a concrete implementation recommendation that fits this repo (IronbellyOrg/IronClaude issue #331, GitHub issue watcher).
Date: 2026-11-07
Sources synthesized: `research/01-claim-flow-and-session-launch.md` (file 01), `research/02-github-surface-and-cli-patterns.md` (file 02), `research/03-governance-and-security-surface.md` (file 03), `research/web-01-github-events-projects-and-limits.md` (web-01), `research/web-02-coder-claude-dispatch-and-security.md` (web-02), `gaps-and-questions.md` (gaps file, 24 numbered gaps, cited here as "Gaps #n"), and the two binding addenda of `research/research-notes.md`.

Binding context applied (research-notes.md, ADDENDUM 2 of 2026-11-07T07:12Z supersedes point 1 of the addendum of 07:03Z): the owner decided D1 = option A (workspace-side poller: each workspace polls GitHub every 5 minutes with its own `gh` login and starts the claim via `orca agent launch`), D2 = claim-only, D3 = only an integer issue number reaches the session prompt (these are the labels of Section 1.1; Qn always means a Section 9.2 open question). The repo-side deliverable is a design doc, a tested poller script/CLI (mock `gh`, mock `orca agent launch`, nothing run live) and trigger/approval label setup instructions; B, C and any live deployment are recorded as follow-up. IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 (workspace listener and registration) was filed assuming a push design; option A makes #477 unnecessary unless B or C is revisited. Options B and C stay evaluated as the documented alternatives and the revisit conditions (scored in Section 6, owner choice recorded in Section 7).

Status tags used below: [CODE-VERIFIED] confirmed against code or a live read-only command by a research file; [CODE-CONTRADICTED] a documentation or earlier-research claim that code contradicts (corrected here and surfaced as a gap); [UNVERIFIED] not confirmed, never presented as fact; [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] confirmed on an official external page by a web research file; [DERIVED] arithmetic or reading derived from cited facts; [PROPOSAL] a design suggestion in a research file, not a fact.


### 3.1 Desired behavior

A watcher selects GitHub issues that are tagged (trigger label), human-approved, in a GitHub Project and in Backlog status, and causes exactly the right developer's Coder workspace to start one new session that runs `/sc:issue --claim <N>`. The issue must also be meant for the polling workspace's `gh` login, so that workspaces do not race for the same issue. The watcher's job ends when the claim session has been started: the claim assigns the issue, optionally assigns the ClickUp task, runs the accuracy check and summary, posts two marked comments and stops at `next-steps.md` (file 01 sections 1 to 3). Everything after the claim (brainstorm, design, workflow, implement) stays manual.

The shape below is the flow the owner's decided option A implies. It is drawn from the research files (file 01 sections 3, 9 and 12; file 02 section 3.3; web-01 section 14.3; web-02 sections A and J); the routing rule and the exact selection query are still design choices (see 3.4 and Section 4).

```
 GitHub (IronbellyOrg/IronClaude)                Coder workspace of developer D
 ---------------------------------               ------------------------------------------------
 issue: trigger label                            poller = plain process (not a Claude session,
        + approval label by a human                      not a worker run), runs every 5 minutes
        + in a Project, Status = Backlog         as D's own `gh` login
        + meant for D's login                    1. read candidates (reads only; no title/body)
                                                 2. check approver, check intended owner
        <------------- gh reads -----------      3. write dispatch record BEFORE launch
                                                 4. build prompt = "/sc:issue --claim " + str(int(N))
                                                 5. orca agent launch --agent claude
                                                      --worktree path:<checkout> --surface terminal
                                                      --prompt <prompt>        (argv list, no shell)
                                                              |
                                                              v
                                                 ordinary top-level Claude session in the checkout
                                                 /sc:issue --claim N  -> assign @me, ClickUp (optional),
                                                 accuracy check, summary, 2 marked comments, STOP
                                                 (next-steps.md is for a person to run)
```

### 3.2 Success criteria (testable pass/fail statements)

All criteria are phrased so that the owner-decided test method (mock `gh`, mock `orca agent launch`, nothing run live) can decide them. File 02 section 5 lists the matching test techniques: pure functions over JSON fixtures, an injectable runner with a recording fake, and one or two PATH-stub end-to-end tests that assert the recorded argv.

| ID | Criterion | Pass/fail test | Research basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| SC-1 | Selection | Given fixture payloads, the poller selects an issue only when it is open, carries the trigger label, is in the Project, has Status Backlog and is approved; each missing condition alone makes it unselected | web-01 section 14.3; file 02 sections 2 and 2b |
| SC-2 | Human approval | An approval label whose latest `labeled` event actor is not an accepted approver does not make an issue selectable. Where the approver list lives is an open decision (Gaps #14) | file 03 section 4; web-01 section 11; web-02 section G2 |
| SC-3 | Intended owner | An issue that is not meant for the polling login (per the chosen routing rule) is never launched; an issue assigned to a login other than the polling login is never launched (the claim would stop with no write, `claim.md:41`) | file 01 section 9; file 02 section 2c |
| SC-4 | Integer-only prompt | The prompt recorded by the mock launcher equals exactly `/sc:issue --claim <positive integer>`, built from `int()` of the `number` field, with no issue-derived text, for fixtures whose title and body contain hostile text | file 01 section 13; file 02 section 3.3 |
| SC-5 | No issue text reaches the poller | No issue title, body or comment text is returned to the poller or reaches a prompt or a log: `gh issue view` and `gh issue list` request only `number,labels,assignees,state`; `gh project item-list`, whose item export carries `title` and `body` (gh source `queries.go:558-566, 673-678`), and the events and comments `gh api` calls are narrowed with `--jq` inside `gh`, so comment text is consumed there. A test feeds hostile `title` and `body` through each fixture and asserts that none reaches the poller's parsed result, audit lines or launch argv | file 02 section 3.3; step 4.5 |
| SC-6 | Argument list, no shell | Both `gh` and `orca` are called with an argument list, never a shell string; a non-integer or non-positive number is rejected before any launch | file 01 section 13; web-02 section F4 |
| SC-7 | Repository pin | Every repository-scoped `gh` call carries `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` (or a `gh api` path spelling `repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/...`); the only exempt calls are `gh api user` and `gh project item-list --owner`, which take no repository (the item repository is checked client-side); the gh-default-repo fallback is never used; no call can resolve to `SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework` | file 02 section 3.3 (conventions A and B); project CLAUDE.md fork rule |
| SC-8 | At-most-once per workspace | A second poll cycle with the same issue does not launch again; the dispatch record is written before the launch; the record survives a restart of the poller process | file 01 sections 10 and 12; web-02 section A4 |
| SC-9 | Lost reply | When the mock launcher simulates the unknown-outcome error (carrying an `operationId`), the poller treats the issue as dispatched and never starts a fresh launch for it; any retry re-issues the same command from the same directory with `--retry-launch <operationId>` only. A launch that times out (no reply, no `operationId`) is treated the same way: no fresh launch, ever; the issue is reported and abandoned after the lease for a person to inspect | file 01 section 12 (rows "Lost reply" and "Other refusals") |
| SC-10 | Caps | A per-cycle launch cap and a per-issue retry cap are enforced (a failed claim that leaves the issue selectable does not re-launch every 5 minutes: it is skipped inside the lease, then retried until the retry cap and reported `abandoned`; an issue with an own claim marker becomes `claimed` and is never retried) | file 01 sections 2 and 10; file 02 section 2c; web-01 section 5 |
| SC-11 | Claim-only | The poller starts only `/sc:issue --claim <N>`; it never runs a next-step command and never starts a pipeline | file 01 sections 3 and 11; Gaps #20 |
| SC-12 | Plain process, never a worker | The poller runs as a plain process outside any Claude session and never as a coordinator-dispatched worker Task | file 01 section 7; file 03 section 5 (WORKER-CONTRACT.md:23, :61) |
| SC-13 | Nothing live | The test suite uses a fake `gh` and a fake `orca`; no test calls GitHub or Orca; a test asserts that no write-shaped `gh` call (`comment`, `edit`, `create`, `-X`, `--add-assignee`, or `--method` with POST, PATCH, PUT or DELETE) is made by the poller; `--method GET` is required on every `gh api` call and is allowed | file 02 section 3.4 (assert_no_retrigger_call pattern); research-notes ADDENDUM 2 |
| SC-14 | Label setup | Trigger and approval label setup instructions exist as `gh label create ... --repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` lines in the house format of `refs/labels.md:22-42`; the poller does not create labels | file 02 section 2b; Gaps #12 |
| SC-15 | Design record | The design doc records the owner decisions D1 to D3, option B and C as documented alternatives with revisit conditions, and IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 as unnecessary under A unless B or C is revisited | research-notes ADDENDUM 2; web-02 section J3 |
| SC-16 | Repo gates | `ruff check src/ tests/`, `ruff format --check src/ tests/`, the pytest suite (run after `make sync-dev`, as CI does, `test.yml:32-38`), `make lint-architecture` and `make verify-deps` pass; the code is Python 3.10 compatible | file 02 section 6 |
| SC-17 | Unverified items stay flagged | The slash-command-as-initial-prompt behavior of `orca agent launch --prompt` is recorded as an owner-run live check, not performed by this work (Gaps #1, #2) | file 01 section 12; file 03 Gaps 4 |

### 3.3 Constraints

| Constraint | Statement | Source |
|---|---|---|
| No live deployment | Nothing in this repo's deliverable deploys or runs live; template wiring (for example a Coder `coder_script`) is Coder-repo follow-up | research-notes ADDENDUM 2; web-02 section A1 |
| Claim identity | The claim assigns `@me`, which is the `gh` login of the running session (`claim.md:47`); no code ties that login to the Coder workspace owner | file 01 section 9; web-02 section B3 |
| Claim-only scope | The watcher's job ends at starting `/sc:issue --claim <N>`; the pipeline after the claim stays manual | research-notes ADDENDUM point 3 |
| Integer-only prompt | Only a validated integer issue number reaches a session prompt, never issue title or body text | research-notes ADDENDUM point 3 |
| Repository target | IronbellyOrg/IronClaude only; the claim resolves the repository from `origin` of the session's working directory, so the launched session must start in a checkout of that repo | file 01 sections 5 and 11 |
| Ordinary session only | `/sc:issue` is on the never-as-worker list (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:23`), so the launched session is an ordinary top-level session; `orca agent launch` must not be called from inside a worker session (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:61`) | file 01 section 7; file 03 section 5 |
| Existing worktree | `orca agent launch` needs an existing worktree (`createsWorktree: false`) and has no `--repo` flag; the checkout is chosen only through `--worktree`; use `path:` or `name:` selectors, not `issue:<n>`, until the Orca link behavior is tested | file 01 section 12; file 03 Gaps 12 |
| Environment | The launched session needs `uv`, `gh auth` for the login, the installed skill, command and issue-worker agents, and a model gateway serving `claude-gw-tier3`, `claude-gw-tier2` and `claude-gw-tier1a`, else the claim stops at Wave 1 with `E-TIER-UNAVAILABLE` | file 01 sections 2 and 11 |
| Python 3.10 and house style | Python 3.10 compatible, stdlib-only preferred, `from __future__ import annotations`, Click group registered after `main.py:561`, design doc in the house format of `docs/designs/sc-issue-command.md` | file 02 sections 4, 6 and 7 |

### 3.4 Consequences of the owner-decided option A (stated as consequences, not as pre-filters)

These follow from the owner's choice of option A. They are not requirements of the issue and do not exclude B or C by assertion; B and C are scored on their merits in Section 6.

| Consequence of option A | What it means for the deliverable | Source |
|---|---|---|
| No listener, no inbound port and no address registry are needed | The owner decided A precisely because it needs none of these; inbound exposure is one of the scored criteria in Section 6 | research-notes ADDENDUM 2 |
| IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 is unnecessary | #477 (workspace listener, monitor registration, secure prompt routing) was filed assuming a push design; under A it is unnecessary unless B or C is revisited. Its listener, registration and transport live in the Coder repo, not in IronClaude | research-notes ADDENDUM 2 and point 2; web-02 section I4 |
| Identity is the workspace's own `gh` login | The poller and the claim both use the same login, so no workspace-to-account registry is needed; the poller should read its login with `gh api user -q .login` rather than assume it equals the Coder owner | file 03 section 7; web-02 sections B3 and recommendation 4 |
| The routing rule is still open | A decided option does not name a routing candidate (assigned to the polling login, per-person label, Project field, or unassigned first-come); the owner decision "names no routing candidate" | file 02 section 2c (last bullet); Gaps #3, #15 |

### 3.5 Out of scope for the target state

| Item | Why | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `/sc:issue --claim --pipeline <level>` | Design-only proposal, not shipped; blocked by the B-CTX gate; claim-only scope was decided by the owner | file 01 section 11; file 03 section 1.3 |
| Running next steps (brainstorm, design, workflow, implement) | Stops by design (B2); manual | file 01 section 11 |
| The listener, registration and transport | Coder repo (#477); unnecessary under A | research-notes ADDENDUM 2 |
| Live deployment, label creation, token scope changes | Setup instructions and follow-up only | research-notes ADDENDUM 2 |

## 4. Gap Analysis

Severity scale: Critical (the target cannot be met or is unsafe without it), Important (the target is met only partly or with unverified assumptions), Minor (documentation, hygiene or a closable question). Every row carries a severity. Current-state cells state only [CODE-VERIFIED] facts or name the unverified item explicitly. "Gaps #n" points to the numbered rows of `gaps-and-questions.md`.

### 4.1 Gap table

| Gap | Current State | Target State | Severity | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1. Missing watcher (the poller itself) | No Python module under `src/superclaude` builds or runs a `gh` call or an `orca agent launch` call (the only Python `orca` calls are `orca skills list` and `orca skills install` in `cli/install_user_setup.py:198-263`). `.github/workflows/` has only `test.yml`, `boundary-guard.yml`, `contract3-generator-constraint-lint.yml` with `push`, `pull_request`, `workflow_dispatch` triggers; no `schedule:`, `issues:` or `workflow_run:` trigger exists [CODE-VERIFIED, file 02 sections 1 and 3.2] | A tested poller CLI that selects, routes and launches (SC-1 to SC-13) | Critical | Package model `src/superclaude/cli/recommend/` (single-file alternative `cli/init_lite.py`); registration is one block after `src/superclaude/cli/main.py:561`; no `pyproject.toml` change (file 02 section 4). Option A needs no workflow (file 02 section 1) |
| G2. Missing Python `gh` runner | None exists. The claim that `src/superclaude/pr_submit/ci.py` is a mockable `gh` subprocess wrapper is [CODE-CONTRADICTED]: it is a pure classifier with no `subprocess` import (`ci.py:10-15`). The only `gh` mention in any `.py` under `src/` is a docstring at `origin_repo.py:14` [CODE-VERIFIED, file 02 sections 3.1 and 3.2; Gaps #22] | A small injectable runner (a function taking an argument list and returning a result) so tests replace it | Important | Nearest patterns: `skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/origin_repo.py` (argv list, `check=False`, `OSError` handling, typed errors, stdlib only; not injectable) and `cli/swarm/tmux.py`. Real `gh` I/O today lives in bash scripts (`skills/sc-pr-submit-protocol/scripts/*.sh`) and protocol prose; the repo mocks `gh` with PATH-stub executables (5 files under `tests/pr_submit/`, file 02 section 3.4). Convention B of those scripts falls back to gh's default repo, which the fork rule warns against; the poller must not copy it |
| G3. Missing `orca agent launch` wrapper and mock | No Python code under `src/superclaude` runs `orca agent launch`; it appears only in docs and specs [CODE-VERIFIED, file 02 section 3.2]. The nearest Python `orca` subprocess precedent is `cli/install_user_setup.py:198-263` (`_find_orca` proves a candidate is Orca before use, because a bare `orca` on Linux is usually the GNOME screen reader; `install_orca_skills` runs `orca skills install` with a timeout) [CODE-VERIFIED, read 2026-11-07]. The argument contract is documented from the installed Orca CLI (file 01 section 12) | An injectable launch function plus a mock that records `--agent`, `--worktree`, `--surface`, `--prompt`, returns exit 0 with JSON, exit 1, and the unknown-outcome error with an `operationId` | Important | Exact JSON key nesting printed by `--json` and the real error body are [UNVERIFIED] (file 01 section 12, Gaps #2). The mock should also simulate a poller started outside an Orca workspace (no `--worktree` default) |
| G4. Missing workspace-to-account binding | Nothing checks that the Coder workspace owner and the workspace's `gh` login are the same person [UNVERIFIED outside this repo, file 03 Gaps 14; Gaps #19]. The claim assigns `@me` = the session's `gh` login [CODE-VERIFIED, `claim.md:47`]. Coder external auth stores a per-user GitHub OAuth token, but the page does not state that the token lookup always uses the workspace owner (web-02 section B3) | Exactly the right developer's workspace acts. Under the decided option A the identity is the workspace's own `gh` login; no registry is needed | Important | The poller should read its login with `gh api user -q .login` each cycle instead of assuming (web-02 recommendation 4). The Coder-side tie is the scope of Coder#477 (row G18), unnecessary under A |
| G5. Missing approval signal | No approval or readiness label, form field or Project field exists. The fork has exactly the 9 GitHub default labels (`gh label list`, 2026-11-07); `labels.md:7` says `/sc:issue` never creates labels and `--claim` changes none; the repo has no approver allow-list. Anyone with Read role can open issues and comment; Triage or higher can apply labels; collaborators counted: 1 admin, 2 maintain, 8 read, 0 triage [CODE-VERIFIED / OBSERVED, files 02 section 2 and 03 section 4.2] | A human-approved state the poller can check, with a verifiable approver | Critical | "Human-approved" can only mean a GitHub-side act by a Triage-or-higher account (3 accounts today). The events API records `actor` for `labeled` events, so the poller can enforce an approver set itself (file 03 section 4; web-01 section 11). Where the allow-list lives is an open decision (Gaps #14). Org owners outside the collaborator list and the permission to edit another user's issue body are [UNVERIFIED] (Gaps #17) |
| G6. Missing intended-owner routing rule | Nothing today decides that an issue is meant for a given workspace's `gh` login. The claim only stops when the issue is assigned to a different login (`claim.md:41`), continues when assigned to the own login (`claim.md:42`) and claims an unassigned issue for whoever runs it (`claim.md:47`) [CODE-VERIFIED]. Analysis finding F3 and accuracy-check row 8 name the rule as missing (file 01 section 8; the accuracy-check file itself is not among the research files read) | The issue must be meant for the polling workspace's `gh` login so that workspaces do not race | Critical | Four candidates are costed in 4.3. The owner decision "names no routing candidate" (file 02 section 2c); choice is a design decision for Section 7. Poller-side predicate supported by the evidence: skip an issue assigned to a login other than the own login (file 02 section 2c) |
| G7. Missing at-most-once guarantee | The claim is check-then-act (lookup `claim.md:26`, edge check `:41-42`, then `--add-assignee @me` `:47`). `--add-assignee` adds and does not replace; GitHub's assignment API is additive with no compare-and-set (web-02 section G1, [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED]). Two logins that both pass the check can both be assigned; the double-claim outcome is derived from code and not observed [UNVERIFIED live, Gaps #3]. The claim comment (`claim.md:88`) is posted only in Wave 6, minutes after the assignment | At most one session per workspace per issue and, in practice, one login per issue | Critical | No documented GitHub primitive gives atomic exclusion across logins (web-02 section G7). The achievable guarantee is best effort: assignee + claim marker comment + per-workspace dispatch record written before launch + a pre-launch re-read of the issue snapshot (a post-write re-read of the assignees belongs only to routing candidate 4, where the poller itself adds `@me`, and is not built under the plan). The real race window (Wave 1 lookup to Wave 3 assign) was not measured (Gaps #15). Re-run safety limits the damage to cost, and across different logins to duplicate comments and double assignment (file 01 section 10) |
| G8. Same-login, several-workspace race | A person with two workspaces polling with one login is not stopped by any assignee check: both pass `claim.md:42`, and both run Wave 1 before either posts in Wave 6, so both post new comments (file 01 section 9). Whether one person has several polling workspaces is [UNVERIFIED] (Gaps #3) | One launch per issue even when one login polls from several workspaces | Important | The dispatch record would then have to live on GitHub, not only locally (file 01 section 9). web-02 section G6 proposes: eligible only if the issue is unassigned or assigned to the own login AND no marker comment of the own login exists AND no live local dispatch exists [PROPOSAL] |
| G9. Claim leaves the issue in Backlog and re-selectable | The claim never reads or writes Projects: Grep for `gh project|projectV2|graphql` over `skills/sc-issue-protocol` found no match [CODE-VERIFIED]. After a claim the issue stays in Backlog status and keeps its labels. A claim that fails after Wave 3 leaves the issue assigned to the own login with no claim comment [derived, file 01 section 10]; a failure before Wave 3 (for example `E-TIER-UNAVAILABLE`) leaves it unassigned and writes nothing (`claim.md:20`) | A launched issue is not launched again every 5 minutes, and a stranded claim is noticed | Important | The two candidate selectors fail differently (file 02 section 2c): selecting "assigned to me" re-selects a post-Wave-3 failure every cycle; selecting "unassigned" strands it. Both need the dispatch record with a retry cap (SC-8, SC-10). Under the plan a `launched` entry is skipped only inside the lease, then retried or reported `abandoned` unless the claim marker exists (then `claimed`); see Phase 5 step 5.1 [DESIGN] |
| G10. Project and Backlog read not possible with the observed token | `gh project list --owner IronbellyOrg` failed with a missing `read:project` scope; the token scopes are `gist, read:org, repo, workflow` [OBSERVED, file 03 section 4.2]. The claim has no Project code | The poller can test "in a Project, Status Backlog" | Important | Documented fixes: classic `read:project`, fine-grained PAT with organization Projects Read, or a GitHub App with the organization "Projects" permission (web-01 section 9); `gh auth refresh -s read:project` is the documented refresh form (web-01 section 13). Org policies on token types are [UNVERIFIED] (web-01 Gaps 7). A label-only selection avoids the question at the cost of the Status check (Gaps #5) |

### 4.3 Intended-owner routing candidates (cost of each, supports rows G6 to G9)

The owner decision names no candidate. The table condenses file 01 section 9, file 02 section 2c and web-01 section 14.4 so Section 6 can score them.

| # | Candidate | What the claim code does with it | Extra setup or cost | Weakness |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Issue already assigned to the polling login (`gh issue list --assignee "@me"`; `assignee:@me` is a documented search and Project filter qualifier, [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED]) | Claim already encodes it: assigned to someone else stops with no write (`claim.md:41`); assigned to self continues, idempotent (`:42`) | No new label; a human approval step must assign the person. Assignee is set by Triage or higher (3 accounts) | Assignee persists after the claim, so it cannot be the dispatch marker; a post-Wave-3 failure is re-selected every cycle; same-login multi-workspace not solved |
| 2 | Per-person label | `--claim` never reads or changes labels (`labels.md:7`) | New label per person created by a maintainer; label names are free text, so the approver check must cover these labels too | Per-person setup; a label write by the poller if it is used as a dispatch marker |
| 3 | Project field (assignee or custom owner field) | No Project code in the claim | Needs the Project read token (row G10); about 9 GraphQL points per 100 items with 8 field values by the documented formula [DERIVED]; server-side filtering by a custom field through REST `q` or `gh --query` is [UNVERIFIED] | Larger response; needs `read:project` on every workspace token |
| 4 | Unassigned, first workspace to take it | Wave 2 passes for an unassigned issue; Wave 3 adds the assignee; no lock | At-most-once record plus a pre-launch re-read | Only candidate with a race: two logins can both pass; a failure after Wave 3 strands the issue (not re-selected) |

The claim code (`claim.md:41-42`) is the strongest evidence |

J3 item 2 is superseded by web-01 section 14.2 |
| `coder ssh` auto-start | web-02 section A3: scheduling page does not say whether `coder ssh` auto-starts a stopped workspace | web-02 section B2: the CLI reference says a stopped workspace starts automatically unless `--disable-autostart` | The CLI reference (primary) corrects the earlier remark; relevant to B and C only |
| Webhook replay and deduplication | web-02 section H1: GitHub's signature page gives no replay protection and does not mention `X-GitHub-Delivery` deduplication | web-01 section 12: the best-practices page tells receivers to track `X-GitHub-Delivery` for deduplication and says a redelivery keeps the original identifier | Different official pages; both stand. ### 4.5 Carried to Section 6 and 7

The gaps that the option scoring must address: G5, G6, G7, G8, G10, G13, G14, G19 (poller host) and G18 (#477). The decided option A needs no listener, which removes the sender-authentication, app-exposure and contract work for A but not for the revisit conditions (Section 5.13 to 5.15).

---

## 5. External Research Findings

All findings in this section are EXTERNAL CONTEXT gathered by the two web research files (web-01 and web-02). Both record `WEB SEARCH PROVENANCE: provider=WebSearch/WebFetch, reason=tavily-unavailable` (no Tavily tool was present in either agent's tool list); WebFetch returned model-written summaries of pages, so quoted wording is the tool's wording, not byte-exact. All pages were retrieved 2026-11-07 and carry no visible publication dates. The codebase is the source of truth: no external finding below overrides verified code behavior (web-01 and web-02 both report that no external finding contradicted a verified codebase finding). Relationship to codebase uses supports, extends or contradicts; "contradicts" does not occur. Facts tagged [UNVERIFIED] are carried to the "Carried to Open Questions" list of Appendix A.2 (and to Section 9) and are not stated as fact. The [UNVERIFIED] items that bear on the decided option A have a Section 9.2 row (including Q34, the ClickUp identity against the `gh` login, and Q35, the minimum `gh` scopes the claim needs); items that concern only the documented alternatives B and C are covered by Q26 to Q29 or stay context only.

Labelling: subsections 5.1 to 5.12 support the decided option A and its de-risking; subsections 5.13 to 5.15 are context for the documented alternatives B and C and for the revisit conditions of option A. Subsection 5.7 and 5.8 serve both.

### 5.1 GitHub Actions `schedule` and `issues` events (context for the documented alternative of an Actions-driven selector, and for A's scheduling comparison)

| Finding | Source URL | Relevance | Relationship to codebase |
|---|---|---|---|
| The shortest interval for scheduled workflows is once every 5 minutes. | Authenticated user primary limit: normally 5,000 REST requests per hour, shared across personal tokens and apps acting for that user. GitHub App installations 5,000 per hour base (cap 12,500 outside Enterprise Cloud); Actions `GITHUB_TOKEN` 1,000 per hour per repository. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api | HIGH | supports: per-developer logins have separate allowances; several workspaces sharing one login share one allowance |
| Secondary limits: up to 100 concurrent requests (REST and GraphQL), 900 points per minute for REST and 2,000 points per minute for GraphQL, most REST reads cost 1 point and writes 5, content creation about 80 per minute and 500 per hour. Response headers `x-ratelimit-limit`, `x-ratelimit-remaining`, `x-ratelimit-used`, `x-ratelimit-reset`, `x-ratelimit-resource`; honor `retry-after`. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | same | MEDIUM | extends: the poller needs backoff and a retry cap (SC-10) |
| GraphQL: 5,000 points per hour per user; cost is the number of connection requests divided by 100 (minimum 1); `first`/`last` 1 to 100; no call may request more than 500,000 total nodes; `rateLimit` object available. GraphQL conditional requests are not documented ([UNVERIFIED]). [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/graphql/overview/rate-limits-and-query-limits-for-the-graphql-api | HIGH | extends: a GraphQL poll pays every cycle |
| Conditional requests: keep the `etag` and send it as `if-none-match`; a `304 Not Modified` consumes no primary allowance when authentication is correct, so idle REST polls can be free. The page recommends webhooks over polling and, when polling is unavoidable, a regular cadence no faster than necessary, honoring `x-poll-interval`, stable filters and ordering. Secondary-limit precautions: sequential calls, at least one second between writes, back off exponentially with a retry cap. Whether the high-level `gh` commands can send `If-None-Match` is [UNVERIFIED]; `gh api` with a header is the plausible route. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/best-practices-for-using-the-rest-api | HIGH | supports: a 5-minute poll is within the "polling when unavoidable" guidance; the official preference for webhooks is the revisit argument for B or C |
| Cost of A [DERIVED by web-01]: one call per 5-minute poll is 12 requests per hour (0.24 percent of 5,000); three calls per poll 36 per hour (0.72 percent); 5 workspaces on one login with 3 calls each 180 per hour (3.6 percent); a Project of 1,000 items at 100 per page is 120 requests per hour in REST (2.4 percent) or about 1,080 GraphQL points per hour (21.6 percent). Exhaustion needs roughly 400 calls per poll, so it is not a realistic revisit trigger under A unless many workspaces share one login or the Project is very large and unfiltered. web-02 section K gives a compatible estimate (10 REST calls per poll is 2.4 percent per login; 5 workspaces on one login 12 percent) [PROPOSAL] | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api | HIGH | supports: confirms option A is affordable on GitHub's side |

Without `--bare`, a `-p` session runs the hooks in a project's `.claude/settings.json` and connects `.mcp.json` servers "even in a folder you've never trusted", with no trust dialog. | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless | HIGH | extends: a headless route must launch only in a checkout the poller controls |

Sweep-style bots were not researched beyond the rows above and no primary page was retrieved; no finding is stated for them (carried to the open-questions list).

Summary of the documented pattern (web-02 section E7): label or assignment is the usual trigger, write access is the usual authority, hidden-content stripping and a single-branch limit appear where the vendor runs the agent, and the closest prior art for binding a GitHub user to a Coder workspace needs a privileged Coder token. Our decided option A (poller on each workspace, workspace `gh` login as identity, approval by an allow-listed labeler [PROPOSAL]) has no equivalent among them because it runs the agent in the developer's own workspace.

### 5.11 Prompt-injection guidance for agents that read untrusted issue text (supports the integer-only prompt; relevant to G12 to G14)

| Finding | Source URL | Relevance | Relationship to codebase |
|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Code security page: permission system (Manual mode requires approval for sensitive operations), network commands not auto-approved, trust verification (a `-p` session shows no trust prompt), sandboxed bash tool, working-directory boundary (an approved Bash command "can still write anywhere your user account can"). Best practices: avoid piping untrusted content directly to Claude; use VMs or containers for isolation; "no system is completely immune". [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/security | HIGH | supports: an integer-only launch prompt does not stop the claim from reading issue text later, and Orca's default `--dangerously-skip-permissions` removes the Manual-mode prompts that this page names as the main protection (file 03 section 2.3); mitigations must not depend on permission prompts |
| OWASP LLM01 Prompt Injection: indirect injection includes content from a website or document (an issue body); mitigations (not prevention): constrain behavior, validate outputs, filter inputs and outputs, apply least privilege (credentials and executable functionality handled in application code), require human authorization for privileged operations, segregate and label external content, adversarial testing. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm01-prompt-injection/ | HIGH | supports: claim-only scope and the human gate after the claim fit "human authorization"; the approver allow-list fits least privilege |
| "Lethal trifecta" (blog post, 2025-06-16, not a standard): access to private data, exposure to untrusted content and the ability to communicate externally together allow leakage; break the combination by removing one leg. Applied to the claim-only session [PROPOSAL]: all three legs are present (issue text read by the claim, the workspace's `gh` token and ClickUp variables, Bash and `gh`); the integer-only prompt removes the untrusted leg only from the launch prompt. | https://simonwillison.net/2025/Jun/16/the-lethal-trifecta/ | MEDIUM | extends: basis for the open policy question in G13 |
| GitHub Actions secure-use guidance: keep attacker-controlled values out of generated shell code, use an intermediate environment variable and double-quote references, grant least-privilege tokens, avoid `pull_request_target` unless needed and never check out attacker-controlled code there, rotate exposed secrets. The worked example is a pull-request title; the principle applies to issue titles and bodies. Applied to the poller [PROPOSAL]: validate the number with a strict integer pattern (for example `^[1-9][0-9]{0,8}$`), pass it as a separate argv item, never interpolate title or body. | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use | MEDIUM | supports: matches SC-4 to SC-6 and the argv-list rule in file 01 section 13 |
| Mitigations supported by the sources [PROPOSAL, web-02 section F5]: integer-only validated argv prompt; approval by an allow-listed account, never by issue content or author alone; least privilege in the session (which `gh` scopes the claim needs is [UNVERIFIED]); human gate after the claim (claim-only scope means no code is changed or pushed); allow-listed tools if a headless route is ever used (`--allowedTools`, `--max-turns`, `--max-budget-usd`); hidden-content hygiene (GitHub strips for Copilot, `claude-code-action` sanitizes; the claim reading the raw body has no such documented filter in our code); post-approval edit risk ([UNVERIFIED]). | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/security and https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm01-prompt-injection/ | HIGH | extends: feeds the Section 6 security criterion and the open decision in Gaps #13 |

### 5.12 At-most-once delivery and claim patterns (supports A; relevant to G7 to G9)

| Finding | Source URL | Relevance | Relationship to codebase |
|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{issue_number}/assignees`: "Users already assigned to an issue are not replaced"; at most 10 assignees per request; without push access assignments are "silently disregarded"; no conditional-request headers, ETags or compare-and-set are documented. Two logins that both pass a "no one assigned" check can both be assigned. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/assignees | HIGH | supports: consistent with the code reading that the claim has no mutual exclusion (file 01 section 9); turns "derived from code" into "consistent with the documented API", though a live double-claim was not observed. A re-read of the assignees reduces but does not close the window [PROPOSAL]; the plan re-reads before the launch, and a post-write re-read would apply only to routing candidate 4 (the poller adds `@me`), which the plan does not build |
| Comments: `author_association` values `OWNER`, `MEMBER`, `COLLABORATOR`, `CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIMER`, `FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR`, `MANNEQUIN`, `NONE`; list endpoints accept `since`; a comment is edited with `PATCH /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/comments/{comment_id}`. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/comments | HIGH | supports: the claim edits its own marked comments (`claim.md:84-88`); the poller can detect "my marker comment exists for issue N" cheaply |
| Idempotency-Key header (IETF Internet-Draft version 08, not an RFC): a retry of a completed request with the same key and fingerprint gets a consistent response, a reused key with a different payload gets 422, a concurrent retry gets 409, key retention is defined by the resource; UUIDs or comparable random identifiers recommended. The key `(repository, issue number, approval event id)` is derived from GitHub, so any workspace computes the same value; it is the natural idempotency key for a push contract (Section 5.15) [PROPOSAL]. Option A does not use it as its record key: the local dispatch record is keyed by `(repository, issue number)` and keeps the approval event id as an attribute, so that a re-approval can be told apart (Section 8, step 5.1) [PROPOSAL]. | https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header/ | MEDIUM | extends: the same key fits a B or C contract (Section 5.15) |
| Leases and fencing (blog post, 2016-02-08): a lease holder can stall past expiry and still act; fencing tokens are needed for correctness; when duplicate work only wastes resources a simple best-effort lock is enough. Mapping [PROPOSAL]: a duplicate claim session wastes a Claude session but damages no data (claim-only scope, idempotent re-claim), so best-effort exclusion is proportionate; a lease with expiry is only a retry throttle (record `dispatched_at`, allow one retry after a stated lease, never more than a cap). | https://martin.kleppmann.com/2016/02/08/how-to-do-distributed-locking.html | MEDIUM | extends: supports SC-10 |
| GitHub Actions `concurrency`: at most one running job or workflow per group; ordering not guaranteed; no exactly-once assurance. It cannot tell several developers' workspaces apart and does not apply to option A, which has no central workflow. | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-jobs/using-concurrency | MEDIUM | extends |

Which pattern gives what (web-02 section G7): assign `@me` is a visible signal but not a lock; the claim marker comment survives workspace loss and is the durable "already claimed" record; a local dispatch record stops repeat launches by one poller only if `$HOME` is persisted; an idempotency key is exact only at a receiver that stores keys; a lease is a retry throttle; Actions `concurrency` serializes one central workflow. Conclusion: no documented GitHub primitive gives atomic exclusion across logins; the achievable guarantee under option A is at most one session per workspace per issue and, best effort, one login per issue [PROPOSAL].

### 5.13 Sender authentication for a push listener (CONTEXT FOR THE DOCUMENTED ALTERNATIVES B AND C AND FOR THE REVISIT CONDITIONS OF OPTION A)

Scope note (repo boundary, web-02 section H): the listener, its registration and its transport live in the Coder repo (IronbellyOrg/Coder#477), not in IronClaude. Nothing in this subsection proposes listener code for IronClaude; it records what a receiver could verify so the design doc can state the revisit conditions. Under the decided option A none of this is built.

| Finding | Source URL | Relevance | Relationship to codebase |
|---|---|---|---|
| Signed requests, GitHub scheme: `X-Hub-Signature-256` is an HMAC hex digest (SHA-256) of the unmodified request body keyed with the webhook secret, prefixed `sha256=`, sent only when a secret is configured; compare in constant time; verify before parsing; keep a high-entropy secret in secure storage. The GitHub page gives no replay protection (no timestamp). [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/validating-webhook-deliveries | HIGH | extends: the repo has no listener or signature code; any sender or receiver is new work (and the receiver is Coder-repo work) |
| Signed requests with replay protection, Stripe scheme: `Stripe-Signature: t=<unix time>,v1=<sig>`; signed payload is timestamp + "." + raw body; HMAC-SHA-256 with the endpoint secret; constant-time compare; reject outside a tolerance (libraries default to 5 minutes; "Don't use a tolerance value of `0`"); ignore schemes other than `v1`; rotation by rolling the secret with the old one active up to 24 hours; duplicates handled by logging processed event ids. Meaning [PROPOSAL]: HMAC over timestamp + body with a per-workspace secret gives sender authentication and bounded replay, but the secret lives on both sides, so a leaked sender secret lets its holder trigger sessions on every workspace that shares it; hence per-workspace secrets. | https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks | HIGH | extends |
| GitHub OIDC tokens issued to Actions workflows: issuer `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`, signing algorithm `RS256`, key set `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com/.well-known/jwks` (from the discovery document, not the docs page); default audience is the repository owner's URL, a custom audience can be requested; the workflow needs `permissions: id-token: write`. Claims a receiver can verify: `sub`, `repository`, `ref`, `workflow`, `job_workflow_ref`, `environment`, `actor`, `repository_owner`, `iss`, `aud`, `exp` (the discovery document adds `iat`, `nbf`, `jti`, `sha`, `run_id`, `event_name`, `repository_visibility`, `runner_environment`, `actor_id`, `repository_id`). "The receiving cloud provider must enforce trust conditions". No shared secret: GitHub signs, the receiver needs only the public key set. Key rotation by GitHub is not described ([UNVERIFIED]). Limit [PROPOSAL]: OIDC proves which workflow in which repo and ref ran and who triggered it, not who approved the issue; the approver check stays with the selector. Fits option B (an Action calls the workspace listener), not option C (a central microservice that is not an Action), in the Section 6 lettering. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/oidc and https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com/.well-known/openid-configuration | HIGH | extends: the repo has no issue-event or schedule workflow, so any OIDC sender is new work in this repo; `claude-code-action` supports OIDC federation too (Section 5.9) |
| Mutual TLS (RFC 8705): the client proves possession of a private key in the TLS handshake; the server matches the certificate by PKI chain plus subject or against a pre-registered self-signed certificate; the sender holds the private key, the receiver holds the CA or registered certificate. Applicability to Coder is not supported by any source found: no page read says the Coder app proxy forwards client certificates to a workspace app or lets a workspace terminate TLS itself ([UNVERIFIED]); traffic reaches a workspace through the control plane or the agent tunnel (Section 5.8). [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED for the RFC] | https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8705 | MEDIUM | extends |
| Coder session or API tokens: header `Coder-Session-Token`; long-lived tokens act for the account holder; the sender holds the bearer secret and Coder verifies it; rotation by creating a new token and deleting the old, lifetime capped by an admin setting; exact status codes for app requests with a bad token are [UNVERIFIED]. Meaning [PROPOSAL]: authentication by Coder token means Coder, not our code, authenticates the sender, the closest fit for "no unauthenticated port exposed", but the token is as powerful as its scope; use narrowly scoped API keys (Section 5.10). [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://coder.com/docs/reference/api/authentication and https://coder.com/docs/admin/users/sessions-tokens | HIGH | extends |
| Webhook delivery facts for a B or C design: `X-GitHub-Delivery` is a GUID per delivery and the best-practices page tells receivers to track it for deduplication, a requested redelivery keeps the original identifier; `X-GitHub-Event`, `X-GitHub-Hook-ID`; payload limit 25 MB; return a 2XX within 10 seconds and do heavier work in a background queue; "GitHub does not automatically redeliver failed deliveries" (redelivery is manual or scripted); the redelivery window and any ordering guarantee are not stated on the pages fetched ([UNVERIFIED]); webhooks need a URL GitHub can reach. [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/validating-webhook-deliveries , https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/handling-failed-webhook-deliveries , https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/best-practices-for-using-webhooks | HIGH | extends: a stopped workspace loses push events unless something redelivers them, which is worse than a pull poller that recovers on restart because state is read from GitHub (web-01 section 14.5) |

Comparison for a B or C revisit [PROPOSAL derived by web-02 section H5 from the sources above]:

| Mechanism | Secret or key holder | Rotation | Replay protection | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HMAC + timestamp | both sides (per-workspace secret) | roll with overlap | yes, by timestamp tolerance (not in GitHub's own scheme) | simple listener, any sender |
| GitHub OIDC | GitHub signs; receiver has the public key set | GitHub-managed | token `exp` plus an idempotency key | sender is a GitHub Action (option B) |
| mTLS | sender private key | certificate reissue | per connection | needs TLS termination at the listener, [UNVERIFIED] through Coder |
| Coder token | sender holds a bearer token; Coder verifies | create and delete tokens, admin maximum lifetime | none by itself | when the listener is a Coder app (Section 5.14) |

Lettering note: the letters in this subsection and in Section 5.1 follow the binding naming of Sections 6 to 9 (B = GitHub Action plus workspace listener, C = central microservice plus listener, NOTES ADDENDUM 2). The web files used other letters for the Action design; Section 6.0 contradiction 1 and Section 9.3 row C10 record that mapping, and Section 4.4 lists it.

With `"authenticated"` any signed-in deployment user's token passes the proxy, so the listener must verify the sender itself (HMAC or OIDC) as defense in depth. | same and the `coder_app` page above | HIGH | extends: Coder#477's requirement "authorized monitor requests reach the intended workspace ... no unauthenticated port exposed" can be built from `coder_app` with `share = "owner"` plus a per-owner token, or from `coder ssh` or `coder port-forward` with a token; each needs a privileged or per-user Coder token held outside the workspace. ### 5.15 Minimal watcher-to-listener contract for B and C, and the conditions to revisit option A (CONTEXT FOR THE DOCUMENTED ALTERNATIVES B AND C AND FOR THE REVISIT CONDITIONS OF OPTION A)

Everything in this subsection is a [PROPOSAL] derived by web-02 section J from the cited sources and the sibling issue's stated requirements. The listener, registration and transport are Coder-repo work (Coder#477); IronClaude would own only the sender (selector and dispatcher) side and this contract text. Under the decided option A the contract is not implemented.

Request (all fixed, validated fields; no title, no body, no comment text, no free-form field, per owner decision D3):

| Element | Content | Documented basis (source URL) | Relevance | Relationship to codebase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Transport | HTTPS POST to a per-workspace listener reachable only through the Coder control plane; authenticated by Coder session or API token and, inside the listener, by a signature or OIDC token | https://coder.com/docs/admin/networking and https://coder.com/docs/reference/api/authentication | HIGH | extends |
| Body | `repository` (string `owner/name`, must equal an allow-listed repository), `issue_number` (integer, 1 to 999999999), `approval_event_id` (integer, the GitHub `labeled` event id from the issue events API), `requested_at` (UTC timestamp), `request_id` (UUID); extra fields rejected, not ignored | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/events and https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm01-prompt-injection/ | HIGH | supports: same integer-only rule as the decided prompt contract (SC-4) |
| Headers | `Coder-Session-Token` (proxy authentication); `Idempotency-Key: <sha256 hex of repository|issue_number|approval_event_id>` (derived, so a retry or a second sender produces the same key); `X-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hmac-sha256 hex>` over `t + "." + raw body`, or `Authorization: Bearer <OIDC JWT>`; receiver tolerance 5 minutes (Stripe's default, a proposed value) | https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header/ , https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks , https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/oidc | HIGH | extends |
| At-most-once mapping | The listener stores `(key to state)` durably (home volume, Section 5.7) and answers a repeat of the same key with the stored outcome, never starting a second session; a claim already made by this workspace's login is also visible on GitHub (claim marker comment) | https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header/ | HIGH | supports: same state tiers as Section 5.7 |

Responses:

| Situation | Code | Notes (source URL for the code where one exists) |
|---|---|---|
| accepted, session start scheduled | 202 | `{"status":"accepted","request_id":...}`; asynchronous start; the sender does not wait for the claim |
| duplicate of a completed request (same key and fingerprint) | 200 | returns the stored outcome (https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header/) |
| same key currently in progress | 409 | same draft |
| same key with a different payload | 422 | same draft |
| missing or invalid signature, OIDC token or Coder token, wrong audience, stale timestamp | 401 | no processing; audit entry (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks returns 400 on verification failure in its example; 401 is the proposal) |
| authenticated but not allowed (sender or approver not on the allow-list, repository not allow-listed) | 403 | audit entry |
| invalid payload (not an integer, out of range, extra fields) | 400 | extra fields rejected |
| workspace not running | none at the listener | the Coder front end answers with an error or the sender sees a connection failure; treat "unreachable" as "not delivered, retry later"; the sender may start the workspace with `coder ssh` auto-start or the build API ([UNVERIFIED] which; https://coder.com/docs/reference/cli/ssh); an idempotent retry is safe |
| listener busy or rate limited | 429 | per-cycle launch cap |

Audit fields to log on both sides (Coder#477 asks for an audit trail of routing and access decisions): `request_id`, `idempotency_key`, `repository`, `issue_number`, `approval_event_id`, approver login (from the event actor), sender identity (Coder user id or OIDC `sub` and `job_workflow_ref`), target workspace id, source address as seen by the proxy, received time, decision (accepted, duplicate, 401, 403, 400, 409, 422), launch outcome, and the Orca launch id or session id if returned.

Conditions under which the owner should revisit option A in favor of B or C [PROPOSAL, web-02 section J3, combined with web-01 section 14.5; each tied to a source or to a gap]. This is the same nine-condition list as the Section 6 table "Revisit conditions for options B and C", which is the authoritative copy cited by Sections 7.8 and 8 and by the design doc. The two research lists are shorter and differently split: web-02 J3 has six items (`research/web-02-coder-claude-dispatch-and-security.md:309-314`) and web-01 section 14.5 has the event-time, change-history and shared-ledger points as separate bullets (`research/web-01-github-events-projects-and-limits.md:178, 181, 182`); items 7 and 8 below merge and renumber them exactly as Section 6 does, and item 9 is derived from G19 and has no research source:

| # | Condition | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Workspaces mostly stopped, so approved issues wait too long for a poll; B or C could wake a workspace through the control plane (`coder ssh` auto-start) at the price of a privileged token | https://coder.com/docs/reference/cli/ssh ; Section 5.7 |
| 2 | Poll latency or GitHub API rate use becomes a problem (many workspaces polling with their own login). Note: web-01 shows the 5,000 per hour allowance is per login, so exhaustion needs many workspaces on one login or a very large unfiltered Project (Section 5.4); web-02's own remark that rate numbers were "not researched" is superseded by web-01 | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api |
| 3 | A need for central audit and a single approver policy that per-workspace pollers cannot enforce consistently (each poller has its own copy of the allow-list) | Gaps #14; G23 |
| 4 | The same login in several workspaces causing duplicate claims that best-effort checks do not prevent; a central dispatcher with one decision point would fix it (R01 §9 gives no estimate for this same-login race, whose window runs to the Wave 6 comment and is longer than the two-login window; the "seconds to minutes, possible but not frequent" estimate in R01 §9 covers only a race between two different logins and is from reading the code, not measured) | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/assignees ; Gaps #3; G8 |
| 5 | Coder ships (or the Coder repo builds) the workspace-to-account binding as a platform feature, making B or C cheap (`create-task-action` shows the pieces exist) | https://github.com/coder/create-task-action |
| 6 | The workspace `gh` token cannot read the needed Project fields while a central service holds a suitable token (GitHub App with organization "Projects" read, user and installation tokens supported) | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/authentication/permissions-required-for-github-apps ; G10 |
| 7 | A requirement to react to a Project Status change at event time (seconds), or to see who moved an issue to Backlog and when: the official best-practices page recommends webhooks over polling; a `projects_v2_item` webhook payload carries the change while a poll sees only current state; whether the issue timeline records a Projects v2 Status change is [UNVERIFIED] | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/best-practices-for-using-the-rest-api ; https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/webhook-events-and-payloads |
| 8 | A need for a shared at-most-once ledger across logins or workspaces; under A only per-workspace state exists and the claim has no mutual exclusion | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/assignees ; G7 |
| 9 | Workspaces cannot run a recurring poller (the `coder_script` cron primitive is not usable in the target template, or state cannot persist) [DERIVED from G19; no research file lists it as a revisit condition and none evaluates an alternative host] | Section 5.7; G19; Q15 |

Facts that cut against revisiting: a scheduled Action cannot use `GITHUB_TOKEN` for an org Project (a PAT or App secret is needed, Section 5.3), may drop runs (Section 5.1), and can only reach a workspace through the same inbound path that B needs, so it removes the polling host but not the listener question; a push design inherits "no automatic redelivery", worse for a stopped workspace than a pull poller (Section 5.13). If none of the nine conditions holds, option A stays and Coder#477 is unnecessary (owner decision).

### 5.16 External Research Summary

| Topic | Key external finding | Effect on the decided option A |
|---|---|---|
| Actions schedule and events | 5-minute floor, delays and dropped runs under load, default branch only, 60-day inactivity disable in public repos; no workflow trigger reacts to a Project Status change; `projects_v2_item` is an organization-level webhook needing an App with "Projects" read | A needs no Action or workflow; B or C would need an App and a reachable endpoint |
| Reading Projects v2 | `read:project` (classic), organization Projects Read (fine-grained or App), not `GITHUB_TOKEN`; REST items route with `q` and `gh project item-list --query` can filter by Status, label and assignee; issue search cannot filter by Status | Each polling login needs `read:project` (the observed token lacks it, file 03 section 4.2); a label-only selection avoids the question; server-side filter acceptance is [UNVERIFIED] until one owner-run live call |
| Cost | 5,000 REST requests per hour and 5,000 GraphQL points per hour per user; a 5-minute poll is 12 calls per hour per call type (0.24 percent); REST `304` is free of primary quota; GraphQL conditional requests undocumented | Negligible for A; exhaustion is not a realistic revisit trigger |
| Approval | `labeled` events expose `actor`; comments and timeline expose `author_association`; Triage or higher can label | An allow-listed-actor check is implementable through REST; allow-list location is an open decision |
| Where the poller runs | `coder_script` with `cron = "0 */5 * * * *"` is the documented in-workspace way; processes stop with the workspace; cron is not "activity"; durable state needs a persisted volume, otherwise GitHub is the only durable record | Host question has a documented mechanism; template wiring is Coder-repo follow-up; a stopped workspace polls nothing |
| Reachability and identity | Agents connect outbound; external systems reach a workspace only through the control plane with a Coder token; external auth stores a per-user OAuth token but the page does not guarantee owner identity | Basis for choosing pull; identity under A is the workspace's own `gh` login, read with `gh api user -q .login` |
| Claude Code | Official page: custom commands expand under `claude -p`; default `-p` permission mode is Manual; `--bare` skips custom commands; Action runs on GitHub runners and its own defenses are explicitly partial | A headless route is a documented-possible alternative; the Orca interactive route is not covered by Claude docs and needs one live check |
| Comparable dispatchers | Label or assignment triggers, write-access authority, hidden-content stripping; Coder's `create-task-action` binds a GitHub user to a Coder user through the OAuth link with a privileged token | Confirms push designs need a privileged token outside the workspace |
| Prompt injection | Least privilege, human authorization, segregation of untrusted content, "no system is completely immune"; permission prompts are not a control under Orca's default skip-permissions | Supports integer-only prompt, allow-listed approvers and claim-only scope; does not remove the claim's own reads (G13) |
| At-most-once | GitHub assignment is additive with no compare-and-set; Actions `concurrency` is not exactly-once; no documented primitive gives atomic exclusion across logins | Best-effort only: assignee, claim marker, dispatch record before launch, pre-launch re-read (a post-write re-read applies only to routing candidate 4, not built), retry cap |
| B and C context | HMAC with timestamp, GitHub OIDC, mTLS, Coder tokens; `coder_app.share` of `owner`, `authenticated`, `public`; a minimal contract with an idempotency key `(repository, issue number, approval event id)`; nine revisit conditions | Recorded as the documented alternatives and revisit conditions; Coder#477 unnecessary unless revisited |

No external finding contradicts a verified code finding. Where web-02 and web-01 differ (rate-limit research, `coder ssh` auto-start, webhook replay and deduplication), Section 4.4 records which source is stronger.

## 6. Options Analysis

**Source key and evidence tags (Sections 6 and 7)**

Every fact below comes from the research files. Citations use these short keys (all paths are under `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RESEARCH-issue-watcher-20261107-064950/` unless noted):

| Key | File |
|---|---|
| R01 | `research/01-claim-flow-and-session-launch.md` |
| R02 | `research/02-github-surface-and-cli-patterns.md` |
| R03 | `research/03-governance-and-security-surface.md` |
