**Integer-only prompt rule recorded in the doc:** the only issue-derived text in any launch argument is `str(number)` where `number` is a Python `int` from `gh` JSON with `1 <= number <= 999999999`; the prompt is the fixed string `/sc:issue --claim ` followed by that integer; it is passed as one element of an argv list, never through a shell (R01 §13; R03 §3.1; W02 F4 pattern `^[1-9][0-9]{0,8}$`, external context https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use). `origin_repo.py` (`src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/origin_repo.py:74` `_NUMBER`, `:102-153` `check_claim`) validates only inside the session after the prompt arrived, so it is a second check, not the first (R01 §5, §13).

### Phase 2: Package skeleton, command runner seam and Click registration

**Goal:** A registered, empty `issue-watch` group and the injectable subprocess runner that every later module takes as a parameter, with tests that pin both.
**Dependencies:** Phase 1 is not a code dependency, but the module list of 8.3 comes from the design doc.

The runner is NEW code: no Python module under `src/superclaude` builds or runs a `gh` call; `src/superclaude/pr_submit/ci.py` is a pure classifier with no `subprocess` import, so the earlier "mockable gh wrapper" claim is [CODE-CONTRADICTED] (R02 §3.1, §3.2). The nearest Python subprocess pattern is `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/origin_repo.py`: `read_origin()` (l.156-174) calls `subprocess.run(["git","remote","get-url","origin"], capture_output=True, text=True, check=False)`, catches `OSError` and non-zero returns and maps them to a typed `ClaimError(code, exit_code, message)` (l.77-83); `main(argv)` (l.177-191) returns an int exit code (R02 §3.2). That file's subprocess call is hard-wired, so the new runner takes the call as a parameter. Precedent for passing a callable as a parameter: `cli/reflect/fallback.py:39` `FallbackTransportFactory = Callable[[str], Transport]`, `cli/audit/tool_orchestrator.py:105`, `cli/audit/batch_retry.py:87` (R02 §3.2). Timeout precedent: `cli/sprint/notify.py:21,25` `subprocess.run(cmd, timeout=5, check=False)`. Precedent for running the Orca CLI from Python: `src/superclaude/cli/install_user_setup.py:198-263` (`_find_orca` proves that a candidate named in `_ORCA_CLIS = ("orca-ide", "orca")` really is Orca by running `skills list --json` and looking for the `orca-cli` topic, because "On Linux a bare `orca` outside Orca's own terminals is usually the GNOME screen reader"; `install_orca_skills` runs `orca skills install` with a deadline-based timeout and `OSError`/`SubprocessError` handling) [CODE-VERIFIED, read 2026-11-07]; the launch function mirrors that proof (step 3.4).

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | Create the package `__init__` with a lazy export | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/__init__.py` (NEW) | First line `"""Issue watcher module -- ``superclaude issue-watch`` CLI command.` (docstring convention of `tasklist/__init__.py:1`, `recommend/__init__.py:1`). Module `__getattr__` that imports `.commands` only when `issue_watch_group` is accessed, as in `recommend/__init__.py:12-20` and `tasklist/__init__.py:9-17`, to keep `superclaude --help` import time down (R02 §4.2) |
| 2.2 | Create the runner seam | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/runner.py` (NEW) | `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CommandResult: returncode: int; stdout: str; stderr: str`. `Runner = Callable[[Sequence[str]], CommandResult]`. `class CommandError(Exception)` with `code: str` and `message: str` (shape of `ClaimError`, `origin_repo.py:77-83`). `def run_command(argv: Sequence[str], *, timeout: float = 60.0, cwd: Path | None = None) -> CommandResult`: `subprocess.run(list(argv), capture_output=True, text=True, check=False, timeout=timeout, cwd=cwd)`, never `shell=True` (`cwd` lets the `orca` runner be bound to the launch directory that Orca's retry rule requires, step 3.4); `FileNotFoundError`/`OSError` becomes `CommandError("E-TOOL-MISSING", ...)`, `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` becomes `CommandError("E-TIMEOUT", ...)`. The 60 second default is a [PROPOSAL]; no research value exists, so the `orca` runner is built with its own, separately settable timeout (also a [PROPOSAL]) and a timeout of `orca agent launch` is never treated as a plain failure (step 3.4) |

No `pyproject.toml` change: `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["src/superclaude"]` (`pyproject.toml:78-79`) already ships any new subpackage (R02 §4.1, §4.2) |

Not needed: `tests/cli/test_cli_registration.py` freezes `EXPECTED_TOP_LEVEL_COMMANDS` but only runs `<name> --help` for each member, so adding a group does not break it; `swarm` and `reflect` are registered yet absent from that set, so the set is a floor and its "forces an update" comment is [CODE-CONTRADICTED] (R02 §4.3).

### Phase 3: Integer-only prompt, `orca agent launch` function and its mock contract

**Goal:** The only code that builds the launch prompt and the only code that calls `orca agent launch`, with the argument contract pinned in a mock that imitates the real CLI as far as the research documents it.
**Dependencies:** Phase 2 (runner seam, `FakeRunner`).

**The `orca agent launch` argument contract the function and the mock must imitate** (R01 §12, from `orca agent launch --help` run locally and the Orca files `specs/agent-launch.js:9-33`, `handlers/agent-launch.js:11-35`, `agent-launch-request.js:38-117`, `agent-launch-recovery.js:54-64`; repo rule in `src/superclaude/core/CLAUDE.md` "Orca session launches" l.45-53 and `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/README.md:228-237`):

| Part of the contract | What the research shows | Tag | What the code and the mock do |
|---|---|---|---|
| Invocation | `orca agent launch --agent <id> [--worktree <selector>] [--prompt <text>] [--model <id>] [--effort <level>] [--surface chat\|terminal] [--focus] [--retry-launch <operationId>] [--json]` | [CODE-VERIFIED] | argv list, never a shell string, where `<orca>` is the executable that `resolve_orca` has proven to be Orca (step 3.4), not a literal `orca`: `[<orca>,"agent","launch","--agent","claude","--worktree","path:<abs>","--surface","terminal","--prompt","/sc:issue --claim <int>","--json"]`. `--model`, `--effort`, `--focus` are not passed |
| Permission mode and allowed tools | The prompt is one free-text argument; `orca agent launch` has no flag for a permission mode and none for an allowed-tool list (flags listed in the Invocation row; `orca agent launch --help`, run 2026-11-07; R03 section 3.1). The launched session's permission mode therefore cannot be set from the launch call; how it is decided per host is open (Section 9.2 Q6; Section 7.9 item 5) | [OBSERVED] (help output); the per-host mode is [UNVERIFIED] | nothing: the mock and the code pass no permission flag |
| Agent id | `--agent` required; `claude` is a known agent (`shared/tui-agent-config.js:15`); unknown agent gives `invalid_argument` | [CODE-VERIFIED] | the mock rejects any other `--agent` value with exit 1 |
| Which worktree or repo | `--worktree <selector>`: `identity:`, `id:`, `name:`, `branch:`, `issue:<n>`, `path:<abs>`, `active`, `current`; omitted/`active`/`current` resolve from the caller's cwd; there is no `--repo` flag | [CODE-VERIFIED] | `path:<abs>` only. `issue:<n>` is not used until the Orca-to-GitHub linking behavior is tested (Q17; project rule forbids Orca link flags for this repo). The mock rejects a non-`path:` selector in the poller's own tests and has a "cwd not inside an Orca workspace" failure for a missing selector |
| Needs an existing worktree | `createsWorktree: false`; a new checkout needs `orca worktree create --agent` | [CODE-VERIFIED] | the function never creates a worktree; `--worktree-path` must be an existing Orca workspace (Q17 for which one) |
| Surface | `--surface terminal` always runs the agent CLI; other values than chat or terminal are refused | [CODE-VERIFIED] | `--surface terminal` always passed |
| How the prompt is passed | `--prompt <text>` becomes `{text, delivery: 'submit'}`: submitted, not left as a draft; an empty or whitespace-only prompt is dropped silently and the agent starts with no prompt; a valueless `--prompt` is rejected | [CODE-VERIFIED] | the prompt is never empty; the mock records the `--prompt` element and the tests assert it equals `/sc:issue --claim <int>` exactly |
| Argv versus typed paste to Claude | Claude's config has `promptInjectionMode: 'argv'`; which path `agent launch` takes lives in the host runtime | [UNVERIFIED] | not modelled; open: Q2 |
| Does a leading `/sc:issue` run as a slash command | not shown by any file | [UNVERIFIED] | not modelled; open: Q1 (one owner-run live test) |
| Agent command | Orca runs the configured Claude command; `ccsession-orca` only when set in Orca Settings (README:216-223); a prompt starting with `/` is read as the task by `ccsession-orca` (`:110` skips `-*` options, `:116-119`) | [CODE-VERIFIED] locally; Coder host [UNVERIFIED] | open: Q5 |
| Success output | text mode lines `agent: terminal`, `handle`, `worktreeId`, `operationId`, `prompt: <outcome>`, `warning`; with `--json` an object `{outcome{kind,handle,sessionId?}, receipt{detail}, worktreeId, operationId, prompt{outcome}, warning}` | fields [CODE-VERIFIED]; exact JSON key nesting [UNVERIFIED] | exit 0 plus JSON on stdout; the code treats exit 0 as "started" and reads `operationId` only if present at the top level; the raw stdout (truncated) goes to the dispatch record. open: Q2 |
| Exit status | error: `reportCliError` then `process.exitCode = 1`; success leaves 0 | [CODE-VERIFIED] by reading, not executed | mock: exit 0 on success, exit 1 on error |
| Lost reply | Documented retry rule (`orca agent launch --help`, run 2026-11-07, last note; same text in `specs/agent-launch.js`): "If the launch reply is lost, re-issue the same command from the same directory on the same host with --retry-launch <operationId> from the error. Never retry as a fresh launch; older hosts require inspecting the workspace first." An unconfirmed outcome throws `agent_session_operation_unknown` with `launchOutcome: 'unknown'`, `retrySafe`, `operationId`, `recoveryCommand`, and the message "the agent may already be running and the prompt may already be submitted" | [CODE-VERIFIED]; real JSON error body [UNVERIFIED] | the mock has an "unknown outcome" mode: exit 1 and a body containing `agent_session_operation_unknown` and an `operationId`; the code recognises it by that code string and keeps the id. The retry re-issues the same command (the original `--agent/--worktree/--prompt`) from the same directory on the same host plus `--retry-launch <id>`, as the documented rule says; a timeout has no reply and so no id, and is never retried as a fresh launch (step 3.4). Only the behavior on the target host stays open: Q3 |
| Other refusals | `incompatible_runtime` when the host cannot start agents; `Setup decision required for this repository` passes through | [CODE-VERIFIED] | the mock has an `incompatible_runtime` mode (exit 1); the code reports `failed` |

|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | Constants and outcome type | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/models.py` (NEW) | `REPO = "IronbellyOrg/IronClaude"` (explicit, never gh's default; R02 §3.3 Convention A and project CLAUDE.md fork rule); `MAX_ISSUE_NUMBER = 999_999_999`; `@dataclass(frozen=True) class LaunchOutcome: kind: Literal["started","unknown","failed"]; operation_id: str \| None; detail: str`. Phase 4 adds the issue models to this file |
| 3.2 | The prompt builder (owner decision D3: integer only) | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/launch.py` (NEW) | `def build_claim_prompt(number: object) -> str`: raise `ValueError` unless `type(number) is int` (rejects `bool`, `str`, `float`, `None`) and `re.fullmatch(r"[1-9][0-9]{0,8}", str(number))`; return `"/sc:issue --claim " + str(number)`. This is stricter than the `str(int(number))` of R01 §13 because `int()` also accepts strings with spaces or non-ASCII digits [PROPOSAL, hardening]; the pattern is W02 F4. The number must come from the `number` field of `gh --json` output (Phase 4), never from title or body |
| 3.3 | Origin check of the target checkout (research finding F5) | same file | `def check_worktree_origin(worktree: Path, git: Runner) -> None`: runs `["git","-C",str(worktree),"remote","get-url","origin"]`, accepts only the two URL forms `parse_origin_url` accepts in `origin_repo.py:66-69` (`https://github.com/o/r[.git][/]`, `git@github.com:o/r[.git][/]`; mirrored, not imported, because skill scripts are installed primitives and not package code), compares owner/repo case-insensitively to `REPO` (as `origin_repo.py:135`), else `CommandError("E-WRONG-ORIGIN")`. Reason: `/sc:issue --claim` reads only the `origin` of the session's working directory and writes `.dev/issues/<N>/` into that checkout; a checkout whose origin is not the fork stops the claim with `E-NO-ORIGIN` or `E-FOREIGN-REPO` (R01 §1, §5, §11; analysis finding F5). Which checkout or Orca worktree in the workspace is used is open: Q17 (no research file shows code that picks one; the CLI takes it as `--worktree-path`) |
| 3.4 | The launch function | same file | `def resolve_orca(orca: Runner, *, which: Optional[Callable[[str], Optional[str]]] = None, names: Sequence[str] = ("orca-ide", "orca")) -> str`: `which=None` is resolved at call time as `lookup = shutil.which if which is None else which`, so a test that patches `shutil.which` takes effect (a default argument bound to `shutil.which` in the signature would be fixed when the module is imported, and a later patch would have no effect; Gate 3 finding F2) [DESIGN]; mirrors `_find_orca` (`src/superclaude/cli/install_user_setup.py:198-221`, with its `_ORCA_CLIS = ("orca-ide", "orca")` at l.35; mirrored not imported because it is private): for each name in `names` it takes `which(name)` (the lookup function is a parameter, so tests never depend on the real `PATH`), runs `[path, "skills", "list", "--json"]` through the injected runner and accepts the path only if the reply is JSON with exit 0 and a topic named `orca-cli`, because a bare `orca` on Linux is usually the GNOME screen reader; a `CommandError` raised by the probe (`E-TIMEOUT` or `E-TOOL-MISSING`) disqualifies that candidate and the loop goes on, as `_find_orca` continues on `OSError` and `SubprocessError` (`install_user_setup.py:217-218`); a probe timeout is therefore a probe failure and is never mapped to `unknown`; none found raises `CommandError("E-ORCA-NOT-FOUND")` [PROPOSAL, mirrors that precedent]. On the research host both candidate names resolve on the real `PATH` (`which -a orca-ide` lists `/home/coder/.local/bin/orca-ide`; `which -a orca` lists `/home/coder/.config/orca/linux-orca-cli-shim/orca` and `/home/coder/.local/bin/orca`; checked 2026-11-07), so a test that used the real lookup could probe or launch the real binary; on a host with no Orca the same test would raise `E-ORCA-NOT-FOUND`. `def build_launch_argv(orca_path: str, number: int, worktree: Path, *, retry_operation_id: str \| None = None) -> list[str]` (requires `worktree.is_absolute()`; `argv[0]` is the resolved Orca path, never a literal `orca`; `"path:" + str(worktree)`; appends `["--retry-launch", id]` when given, original arguments kept, as the documented retry rule says: "re-issue the same command from the same directory on the same host"). `def launch_claim(number: int, worktree: Path, *, orca: Runner, retry_operation_id: str \| None = None, which: Optional[Callable[[str], Optional[str]]] = None) -> LaunchOutcome` (`which` defaults to `None`, is resolved to `shutil.which` at call time inside `resolve_orca`, and is passed to it; `run_cycle` takes the same optional `which` parameter and passes it on, step 5.2): resolves Orca, builds the argv with `build_claim_prompt(number)`, calls `orca(argv)`; the `orca` runner is bound to `cwd=worktree` by `build_runners(worktree)` (step 5.3), so every launch and every retry runs from the same directory, the one stored in the dispatch record (step 5.1). Exit 0 gives `started` (operation id read tolerantly from the JSON); non-zero with `agent_session_operation_unknown` in stdout/stderr gives `unknown` with the id; any other non-zero gives `failed`. `CommandError("E-TOOL-MISSING")` or `E-ORCA-NOT-FOUND` (no process was started) gives `failed`. **`CommandError("E-TIMEOUT")` raised by the launch call itself (not by the `resolve_orca` probe) gives `unknown` with `operation_id=None`, not `failed`**: a timeout is a lost reply, the session may already be running, and the launch spec says "Never retry as a fresh launch". The function never retries by itself and never launches fresh after `unknown` |
| 3.5 | The mock | `tests/issue_watch/conftest.py` (extend), `tests/issue_watch/fixtures/orca-launch-ok.json`, `orca-launch-unknown.json`, `orca-launch-incompatible.json` (all NEW) | `class FakeOrca(FakeRunner)` implements the contract table: validates the argv shape (subcommand `agent launch`, `--agent claude`, `--worktree path:<abs>` with an absolute path, `--surface terminal`, a non-empty `--prompt`), then returns the scripted mode: `ok` (exit 0, JSON), `error` (exit 1), `unknown` (exit 1 with `agent_session_operation_unknown` and `operationId`), `incompatible` (exit 1), `no_workspace` (exit 1, for a missing `--worktree`), `timeout` (raises `CommandError("E-TIMEOUT")`, a lost reply). It also answers the `skills list --json` probe of `resolve_orca` with `{"topics":[{"name":"orca-cli"}]}` (the shape `_find_orca` reads, `install_user_setup.py:216-219`), and has a `not_orca` mode that answers the probe with non-JSON, as a screen-reader `orca` would, and a `probe_timeout` mode in which only the probe raises `CommandError("E-TIMEOUT")` (in `timeout` mode the probe is answered normally and only the launch call times out). Fixture JSON shapes are marked in a comment as "assumed from R01 §12, replace after the live test (Q2)" |
| 3.6 | Tests for the prompt and the launch contract | `tests/issue_watch/test_prompt_validation.py` (NEW), `tests/issue_watch/test_launch_contract.py` (NEW) | Prompt: parametrized accept `1`, `331`, `999999999`; reject `0`, `-1`, `1000000000`, `True`, `3.0`, `None`, `"331"`, `"331 extra"`, `"#331"`, `" 331"`, a non-ASCII digit string, and a string such as `"331; echo x"`. Contract: the recorded argv equals the list in the contract table exactly; the `--prompt` value is a single element equal to `/sc:issue --claim 331`; the only issue-derived element of the argv is that integer; the runner was called with a list; `ok` gives `started`; `error` and `incompatible` give `failed`; `unknown` gives `unknown` with the id and a second call with `retry_operation_id` contains `--retry-launch <id>` and otherwise the same argv; `timeout` gives `unknown` with no id (never `failed`); `resolve_orca` (always called with a fake `which=lambda name: "/fake/" + name` or a dict lookup, never the real `shutil.which`, so the result does not depend on whether Orca is installed on the test host) accepts a candidate that answers the probe with the `orca-cli` topic, skips one that answers with non-JSON or a non-zero exit, skips one whose probe raises `CommandError("E-TIMEOUT")` (a probe failure: the result is the next candidate or `E-ORCA-NOT-FOUND`, never `unknown`), prefers `orca-ide` over `orca`, and raises `E-ORCA-NOT-FOUND` when none qualifies (no launch call is made); `launch_claim` with a timeout of the probe (`FakeOrca` mode `probe_timeout`) gives `failed` when no other candidate qualifies, while a timeout of the launch call itself gives `unknown` (the `timeout` mode of `FakeOrca` applies to the launch call only); a relative worktree path raises. `check_worktree_origin`: accepts both URL forms for `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` in either letter case; rejects another owner, rejects a URL containing `SuperClaude-Org`, rejects a failing `git` |

**Mocking style for these tests:** injected runner (`FakeOrca`, `FakeRunner`), because the unit under test takes the runner as a parameter. The repo's existing way of mocking `gh` is different and is used in Phase 6: a stub `gh` executable placed on `PATH` and run through `subprocess.run` (for example `tests/pr_submit/test_conflict_e2e.py`, which builds `["gh", *args]` and runs it against a PATH stub; four other `tests/pr_submit/` files write `gh` stubs too: R02 §3.4).

### Phase 4: Read-only GitHub queries and the five-filter selection

**Goal:** Pure selection logic with five filters plus a thin read-only `gh` layer. No write-shaped `gh` call exists anywhere in the package, and no `gh` read returns issue title, body or comment text to the poller: `--json` lists contain none of them, and the calls whose output carries them (`project item-list`, the events and comments `api` calls) are narrowed with `--jq` inside `gh`.
**Dependencies:** Phase 2 (runner, `FakeRunner`). Independent of Phase 3 except for the shared `models.py` file (Phase 3 creates it; this phase extends it).

**The five filters, each applied through the cheapest correct query the research found** (W01 §14.3, external context; R01 §9; R02 §2c; R03 §4):

| # | Filter | Rule | Where it is evaluated | Extra `gh` calls | Evidence and open points |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Tagged | the trigger label is on the issue and the issue is open | server side in the discovery query, then re-checked client-side on the `issue view` snapshot | 0 beyond the snapshot | label names do not exist yet (fork has only the 9 default labels, R02 §2): names are required options, open: Q9 |
| 2 | Human-approved | the approval label is currently on the issue, and the latest `labeled`/`unlabeled` event of that label is a `labeled` event whose `actor.login` is on the approver allow-list | client side, only for issues that passed filters 1, 3, 4, 5 | 1 or more events calls per survivor (pages of 100, up to the page cap; reaching the cap fails closed: step 4.2) | actor of the label event: W01 §11, W02 G2 (external context https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/events); Triage role or higher can label, so role alone is too weak (R03 §4.2); allow-list location and rule open: Q10; edit-after-approval not detectable: Q11 |
| 3 | In the Project | the issue is an item of the configured Project | server side: the Project items query is the discovery query | 0 | needs `read:project` on the poller's login, which the observed token lacks (R03 §4.2): Q7; `--label-only` mode drops filters 3 and 4 and is a degraded mode the owner must allow: Q9 |
| 4 | Status Backlog | the item's Status is Backlog | server side `status:Backlog` in `--query`; client side fail closed: an item whose status cannot be read from the JSON is rejected | 0 | The gh manual page for `gh project item-list` shows status filters with `--query` in its examples (`assignee:octocat -status:Done`, `label:bug -status:Done`; [EXTERNAL-VERIFIED] and [CODE-VERIFIED by running `gh project item-list --help`, gh 2.103.3]); whether the API host accepts exactly `status:Backlog`, and the JSON schema, remain [UNVERIFIED]: Q8. The claim leaves the item in Backlog (R01 §3), so the dispatch record, not the status, prevents repeats |
| 5 | Intended owner | the issue is assigned to the own login and to nobody else | server side `assignee:@me` in the query, client side on the snapshot's `assignees` | 0 | routing candidate 1 of R01 §9 / S03 §7.4, the one the research supports; the claim stops when the issue is "assigned to anyone other than the current login" (`refs/claim.md:41`) and continues when assigned to the current login (`:42`), so the shared predicate "skip an issue assigned to a login other than the own login" holds (R02 §2c). The owner has not chosen the routing rule: open: Q4. The predicate is one replaceable function |

Calls the package makes (argv after the program name; all read-only). **The read rule, stated once:** (1) every `gh api` call carries `--method GET`, the pattern of `skills/sc-pr-submit-protocol/scripts/poll-augment-review.sh` l.53-55 `--method GET -F per_page=100 -F page=...` (R02 §3.3); (2) every repository-scoped call (`issue view`, `issue list`, and the events and comments `gh api` calls) is pinned to the fork, by `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` or by a `repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/...` path; (3) exactly two calls are exempt from the repository pin, because they take no repository: `gh api --method GET user -q .login` (reads the caller's own login) and `gh project item-list <N> --owner <project-owner>` (reads an organisation Project; the content repository of each item is checked client-side and an item without a readable repository is rejected); (4) `--method` is forbidden only with POST, PATCH, PUT or DELETE, and no write verb (`edit`, `comment`, `create`) or `-X` appears in any argv. Tests 4.5 and 6.2 and the stub of 6.1 apply exactly this rule.

| Call | argv | Returns / projects | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Own login | `gh api --method GET user -q .login` (the claim's own form is `gh api user -q .login`, `refs/claim.md:26`; `--method GET` is added so that every `gh api` call follows rule (1)) | the login, validated against `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]*` before it is used in a jq string [PROPOSAL] | R01 §9; `refs/claim.md:26` |
| Discovery, Project mode | `gh project item-list <N> --owner <project-owner> --query "<q>" --format json -L 100 --jq <items-jq>` where `<q>` is built only from operator options and fixed words: `is:issue is:open status:Backlog label:<trigger> assignee:@me` (`is:open` keeps closed issues that were left in Backlog out of the result; `is:issue is:open` is the form of the `gh project item-list --help` example), and `<items-jq>` projects each item to `{number, repository, status}` only, for example `[.items[] \| {number: .content.number, repository: .content.repository, status: .status}]` [PROPOSAL; the key names are assumed and open: Q8]. **`--jq` is mandatory:** the item export of `gh project item-list` carries each issue's `title` and `body` (gh source `pkg/cmd/project/shared/queries/queries.go`, read through `gh api repos/cli/cli/contents/...` on 2026-11-07: `Issue.ExportData` exports `body`, `title`, `number`, `repository`, `url` at l.558-566, and `ProjectItem.ExportData` exports `id`, `title`, `body`, `type` at l.673-678), so without the projection public-authored text would enter the poller's stdout on every cycle | issue numbers (must be `int`) of items whose content repository equals `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`; an item without a readable repository is rejected, because the Project may hold issues of other repos and `issue view <N> --repo` would otherwise read a different issue with the same number [PROPOSAL, fail closed; JSON schema unverified: Q8] | W01 §7, §8 (external context https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_project_item-list); default `-L` is 30, so `-L 100` is explicit |
| Discovery, label-only mode | `gh issue list --repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude --label <trigger> --assignee @me --state open --json number --limit 100` | issue numbers | flags verified by running `gh issue list --help` (R01 §9, R02 §2c) |
| Snapshot | `gh issue view <N> --repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude --json number,state,labels,assignees` | `Candidate` | subset of `refs/claim.md:26`; title, body and comments are never requested (R02 §3.3) |
| Label events | `gh api --method GET repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/issues/<N>/events -F per_page=100 -F page=<p> --jq <events-jq>` | per page `{n, items}`: the raw page length and the `LabelEvent` list (id, event, actor, label name) of that page | W01 §11, W02 G2. The REST events list comes back oldest first (read-only `gh api` call on issue #331 on 2026-11-07: first event 2026-10-30, last 2026-11-07), so a page cap drops the newest events; the list is read to its end or the read fails (step 4.2). The repo's own precedent is `poll-augment-review.sh:47` ("fail incomplete at the cap, never silently truncate") with 1000 pages (`:51`) |
| Own claim marker | `gh api --method GET repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/issues/<N>/comments -F per_page=100 -F page=<p> --jq <marker-jq>` | per page `{n, items}`: the raw page length and the list of comment ids only | marker `<!-- sc:issue:claim -->` and the own-login rule of `refs/claim.md:29, :84-88`; field name of the comment author in the REST object is not shown by the research (it shows `author_association` and `since`): open: Q11 |

```
events-jq : {n: length, items: [.[] | select(.event=="labeled" or .event=="unlabeled") | {id, event, actor: .actor.login, label: .label.name}]}
marker-jq : {n: length, items: [.[] | select(.user.login == "<own-login>") | select(.body | contains("<!-- sc:issue:claim -->")) | .id]}
```

Each filter returns the raw page length `n` (taken before any `select`) together with the filtered `items`. The page-end test uses `n`, never the length of `items`, because a raw page of 100 events that holds 40 label events comes back with 40 items: counting the filtered items would read that full page as a short last page and stop early on partial data [DESIGN, the logic of the plan itself; the repo precedent tests the raw page, `poll-augment-review.sh:69-70`: `count="$(jq 'length' ...)"` then `if [ "$count" -lt 100 ]` ends the read, and `:74` fails when the end was not reached].

The comment text is consumed inside `gh --jq` and only ids leave it, so no comment body reaches the poller or any prompt (owner decision D3).

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | Issue models | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/models.py` (extend) | `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Candidate: number: int; state: str; labels: frozenset[str]; assignees: tuple[str, ...]`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) class LabelEvent: event_id: int; event: str; actor: str; label: str`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Decision: number: int; selected: bool; rejected_by: str \| None; detail: str`. `@dataclass(frozen=True) class SelectionConfig: trigger_label: str; approval_label: str; approvers: frozenset[str]; own_login: str` |
| 4.2 | The read-only GitHub layer | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/github.py` (NEW) | `def whoami(gh: Runner) -> str`; `def discover_project(gh: Runner, *, owner: str, project_number: int, trigger_label: str) -> list[int]`; `def discover_label_only(gh: Runner, *, trigger_label: str) -> list[int]`; `def snapshot(gh: Runner, number: int) -> Candidate`; `def label_events(gh: Runner, number: int, *, label: str, max_pages: int = 5) -> list[LabelEvent]`; `def own_claim_comment_ids(gh: Runner, number: int, *, login: str, max_pages: int = 5) -> list[int]`. Each builds `["gh", ...]` as in the table above and calls the injected `gh`; a non-zero return or invalid JSON raises `CommandError("E-GH-FAILED")`. Every number placed in an argv is first checked with `type(n) is int`. `max_pages = 5` is a [PROPOSAL]; no research value. **Fail closed on truncation:** the events and comments lists come back oldest first, so a cap would drop the newest entries. Each page is read through the jq filters above, which return `{n, items}` with `n` the RAW page length before filtering. The loop reads page 1, 2, ... and stops with a complete list when a page has `n < 100`; it continues while `n == 100`. If page `max_pages` is read and its `n` is still 100 (the end of the list was not reached), `label_events` and `own_claim_comment_ids` raise `CommandError("E-GH-TRUNCATED")`; they never return a partial list. The test is on `n`, not on `len(items)`: a filtered page is usually shorter than 100 even when the raw page is full, so a test on the filtered length could never fire and would read a short filtered page as the end of the list. A page whose output is not an object with an integer `n` and a list `items` raises `CommandError("E-GH-FAILED")` [PROPOSAL; same rule as `poll-augment-review.sh:47`, which fails incomplete at its page cap, and the same raw-count test as `poll-augment-review.sh:69-70`]. A module-level `READ_ONLY_VERBS` check is not added: read-only behavior is asserted in tests (step 4.5), where the repo's own precedent for it lives (`assert_no_retrigger_call`, `tests/pr_submit/test_conflict_e2e.py:178-186`) |
| 4.3 | The pure selection logic | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/selection.py` (NEW) | `def passes_cheap_filters(c: Candidate, cfg: SelectionConfig) -> Decision` (filters 1, 5 and open state; filters 3 and 4 are decided by discovery and recorded by the caller). `def is_intended_owner(c: Candidate, own_login: str) -> bool` (assignees equal to `(own_login,)`; the single replacement point for the routing rule, Q4). `def is_approved(events: Sequence[LabelEvent], cfg: SelectionConfig) -> Decision` (latest event for `cfg.approval_label` by `event_id` order; approved only if it is `labeled` and its actor is in `cfg.approvers`; the approval label must also be in the snapshot's labels). `is_approved` is called only with a complete event list: the cycle maps `E-GH-TRUNCATED` from `label_events` to `Decision(selected=False, rejected_by="approval-unverifiable")`, so approval is never decided on partial data (fail closed). `def load_approvers(path: Path) -> frozenset[str]` (one login per line, `#` comments; an empty or missing file raises `CommandError("E-NO-APPROVERS")`, so a missing list never approves everything; the file format and location are a [PROPOSAL]: open Q10). No `gh` call in this module |
| 4.4 | Fixtures | `tests/issue_watch/fixtures/issue-view-*.json`, `events-*.json`, `project-items.json`, `list-numbers.json`, `comments-marker.json` (all NEW) | Hand-written JSON in the shape `gh` documents for the fields requested. The events and comments fixtures are RAW REST pages (full event or comment objects, before any jq filter) so the filters and the raw page length `n` are exercised (step 4.5). `project-items.json` carries a header comment-field `"_assumed_shape": "replace after the owner-run live call (Q8)"` so the assumption is visible |
| 4.5 | Selection and read-only tests | `tests/issue_watch/test_selection.py` (NEW), `tests/issue_watch/test_github_reads.py` (NEW) | Selection (pure, JSON fixtures, the style of `tests/pr_submit/test_ci_classify.py`, R02 §5): each of the five filters rejects on its own with `rejected_by` naming it; an issue assigned to another login is rejected; assigned to own login plus another is rejected; unlabeled after labeled is not approved; labeled by a non-allow-listed actor is not approved; the latest event wins when labeled, unlabeled, labeled again; a missing allow-list file raises; truncation fails closed, and the tests exercise the jq filter rather than skip it: the fixtures are RAW pages of 100 events that hold fewer than 100 label events (for example 40 `labeled`/`unlabeled` events among 60 other events), and the `FakeRunner` serves the page through the same filter semantics as `events-jq`/`marker-jq`, giving `{n: 100, items: [40 entries]}` (the unit-level `FakeRunner` helper applies the filter string it receives in `--jq` to the raw fixture page with `jq -c`, so a wrong filter fails a test; the PATH stub does the same in Phase 6, step 6.1) [DESIGN]. A `FakeRunner` that returns such a raw page of 100 (filtered to fewer than 100 items) for every page up to `max_pages` makes `label_events` raise `E-GH-TRUNCATED`, and the same for `own_claim_comment_ids`; a raw last page of 100 events whose filtered `items` are fewer than 100 does NOT end the read (only `n < 100` does); a raw last page with `n < 100` is read to its end and decided normally (the cycle-level assertion is in `test_cycle.py`, "Truncated read" below). Reads: with `FakeRunner`, every recorded argv starts with `gh`; contains `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` or a `repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/` path or is `api user` or `project item-list --owner`; every `gh api` call has `--method GET`; no `--json` list contains `title`, `body` or `comments`; the Project discovery argv contains `--jq`, and `project-items.json` carries a hostile `title` and `body` (as gh's item export does) that never appear in the parsed discovery result, the audit lines or any recorded argv; no argv contains `SuperClaude-Org` or a `{owner}` placeholder; no argv has a whole token equal to `edit`, `comment`, `create`, `-X` or `--add-assignee`, and none uses `--method` with POST, PATCH, PUT or DELETE (the token list is modelled on `assert_no_retrigger_call`; a path such as `repos/.../comments` is one token and is allowed because it is a GET read); the `-L`/`--limit` value is explicit and 100; a non-integer in a discovery result is dropped and reported; an item with another repository is dropped |

### Phase 5: Dispatch record, at-most-once guards, the poll cycle and the `once` verb

**Goal:** One complete poll cycle: lock, identity, discovery, five filters, GitHub-side and local at-most-once checks, dispatch record written before the launch, launch through the Phase 3 function, and an audit line per decision. Exposed as `superclaude issue-watch once`.
**Dependencies:** Phases 2, 3 and 4.

**What at-most-once can and cannot be under this design** (R01 §9, §10; R03 §7; W02 G1, G7 table): the claim is check-then-act and `--add-assignee @me` adds rather than replaces, so no atomic exclusion exists across logins; GitHub assignment has no compare-and-set (external context https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/assignees). The achievable guarantee is: at most one launch per workspace per issue within the lease and the attempt cap, plus best-effort exclusion across logins (intended-owner filter, own claim marker, pre-launch re-read at step 8 of the algorithm below). A post-write re-read of the assignees (W02 G7 [PROPOSAL]) would exist only for routing candidate 4, in which the poller adds `@me` itself; this plan's poller never writes to GitHub (SC-13, step 4.5), so that layer is not built. The claim comment is posted last (Wave 6, `refs/claim.md:84-88`), minutes after the assignment (Wave 3, `:47`), so the marker is a late record and the local record covers the gap. A claim that fails after Wave 3 leaves the issue assigned to the own login with no marker, and a claim that stops before Wave 3 (for example `E-TIER-UNAVAILABLE`, `claim.md:20`) writes nothing, so the issue keeps matching the filters. The dispatch record skips such an issue while the lease runs, then retries it, and the attempt cap and an `abandoned` report end the retries; an issue whose marker exists becomes `claimed` and is never launched again (state table in step 5.1; R01 §10, R02 §2c).

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | The dispatch record and the lock | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/state.py` (NEW) | `@dataclass class DispatchEntry: repository: str; issue: int; state: Literal["pending","launched","unknown","failed","abandoned","claimed"]; attempts: int; first_at: str; last_at: str; operation_id: str \| None; launch_cwd: str \| None; approver: str \| None; approval_event_id: int \| None; detail: str`. **Key rule:** the record is keyed by `(repository, issue number)` (the poller handles one repository, `REPO`, so the stored key is the string `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude#<N>`); the approval event id is an attribute of the entry, not part of the key, so that a re-approval (a newer `labeled` event on an issue that already has an entry) can be told apart and reported as `re-approved` in the audit line; a re-approval does not reset `attempts` and does not allow a fresh launch by itself (the own-claim-marker check of algorithm step 6 and `may_dispatch` still decide); whether a re-approval should reset the record is an owner decision (open: Q13). `launch_cwd` is the directory the launch ran from (the `--worktree-path`), stored so that a retry runs from the same directory. `class DispatchStore: __init__(self, path: Path); load(self) -> dict[str, DispatchEntry]; save(self, entries: dict[str, DispatchEntry]) -> None` writing a temp file in the same directory and `os.replace` (atomic write via temp file plus rename, W02 A4; same technique as `_atomic_write` in `src/superclaude/cli/init_lite.py` l.221, R02 §4.2, mirrored not imported). A corrupt or missing file loads as empty and the GitHub-side marker check still protects (below). The state file also holds a `considered` map `{key: ISO time}`, the last cycle in which the issue was examined at step 4 whatever the outcome (including rejections such as not yet approved): `DispatchStore.load_considered(self) -> dict[str, str]` and `record_considered(self, key: str, at: str) -> None`; step 3 sorts by it so that issues that stay unhandled cannot monopolize the candidate cap [DESIGN, Gate 3 finding F1]. `@contextmanager def poll_lock(path: Path) -> Iterator[bool]`: `fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX \| LOCK_NB)` on `<state-file>.lock`; yields `False` when another run holds it (overlap of a slow run with the next tick is [UNVERIFIED] for `coder_script` cron: W02 A1, Q15). `def may_dispatch(entry: DispatchEntry \| None, *, now: datetime, lease: timedelta, max_attempts: int) -> tuple[str, str]` returning an action (`launch`, `retry-launch`, `wait`, `abandon`) and a reason: no entry gives `launch`; **the `wait` window, stated once:** `pending`, `launched` and `unknown` entries give `wait` while `now - last_at < lease` and become eligible again after it [DESIGN]; after the lease `pending` and `launched` give `launch` while `attempts < max_attempts`, else `abandon`; `unknown` with a stored id gives `retry-launch` after the lease (same command, run from the stored `launch_cwd`, which must equal the current `--worktree-path`, else `abandon` with reason `cwd-changed`) while `attempts < max_attempts`, else `abandon` and never a fresh `launch`; `unknown` with no id (a timed-out or otherwise unconfirmed launch, step 3.4) gives `wait` until `now - last_at >= lease`, then `abandon` so that a person inspects the workspace first ("older hosts require inspecting the workspace first", launch help text), and never `launch`; `failed` gives `launch` while `attempts < max_attempts`, else `abandon`; `abandoned` and `claimed` give `wait` (both are terminal). A `pending` record means the process may have died after the launch started, so it is treated like `launched` (the launch reply can be lost after the session started: R01 §12). `launched` records that Orca started the session, not that the claim finished: a claim that stops after the launch (for example `E-TIER-UNAVAILABLE`, which writes nothing, `claim.md:20`) leaves an issue that carries no claim marker, so after the lease that entry is retried by `launch` or reported as `abandoned`, and it is never hidden for good. `claimed` is set only by step 6 of the cycle when the own claim marker comment exists (the claim comment carrying the marker is the last write of the claim, Wave 6 item 2, `claim.md:88`); it is the only state that ends handling without a person. The cycle applies this one table in two places: step 3 (before the candidate cap) drops `claimed` and `abandoned` always and drops `pending`, `launched` and `unknown` only while inside the lease, and step 7 decides the entries that remain [DESIGN]. Defaults [PROPOSAL, the research gives no value for any of these]: lease 60 minutes, `max_attempts` 3, `max_launches` per cycle 1 (W02 recommendation 7 asks for a cap and a retry cap without numbers): open Q13 |
| 5.2 | The poll cycle | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/cycle.py` (NEW) | `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CycleConfig: worktree: Path; trigger_label: str; approval_label: str; approvers: frozenset[str]; project_owner: str \| None; project_number: int \| None; label_only: bool; max_candidates: int; max_launches: int; max_attempts: int; lease: timedelta; dry_run: bool`. `@dataclass class CycleReport: own_login: str; decisions: list[Decision]; launched: list[int]; waiting: list[int]; abandoned: list[int]; claimed: list[int]; failed: list[int]; dropped: list[str]`. `def run_cycle(cfg: CycleConfig, *, gh: Runner, git: Runner, orca: Runner, store: DispatchStore, now: Callable[[], datetime], which: Optional[Callable[[str], Optional[str]]] = None) -> CycleReport` (`which` defaults to `None`, meaning `shutil.which` resolved at call time, and is handed to `launch_claim` at step 12; the unit and cycle tests pass a fake lookup, so they never read the real `PATH`). The ordered algorithm is the table below |
| 5.3 | The `once` verb and exit codes | `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/commands.py` (extend step 2.3) | `@issue_watch_group.command("once")` with the options below. `def build_runners(worktree: Path) -> tuple[Runner, Runner, Runner]` returns `(gh, git, orca)` wrappers of `run_command` (the `orca` one bound to `cwd=worktree` and to its own timeout, step 2.2) and is the seam tests replace with `monkeypatch.setattr`, as `tests/pr_submit/conftest.py:58` patches `detection._fetch_payload` (R02 §3.1). The verb takes the lock, builds `CycleConfig`, calls `run_cycle`, prints one JSON object per decision to stdout (the audit line) and exits with the table's code through `sys.exit(...)` (the `cleanup_audit/commands.py` style, R02 §4.2). `--dry-run` runs the read-only GitHub calls, then prints what would be launched and returns before any record write or launch, the precedent of `cleanup_audit/commands.py:85-87` (`_print_dry_run`, R02 §4.2) |
| 5.4 | State and cycle tests | `tests/issue_watch/test_state.py` (NEW), `tests/issue_watch/test_cycle.py` (NEW), `tests/issue_watch/test_cli_once.py` (NEW) | See the scenario list below |

**Ordered algorithm of `run_cycle`:**

| # | Action | On failure or skip |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `check_worktree_origin(cfg.worktree, git)` (step 3.3) before any GitHub call | `CommandError("E-WRONG-ORIGIN")`, exit 1 |
| 2 | `own_login = whoami(gh)` | exit 2 |
| 3 | Discovery (`discover_project` or, with `--label-only`, `discover_label_only`); keep only values with `type(n) is int` and `1 <= n <= MAX_ISSUE_NUMBER`, report the others in `dropped`; **then drop, with the table of step 5.1, every number whose local entry is `claimed` or `abandoned` (always) or `pending`, `launched` or `unknown` while `now - last_at < lease` (a cheap local skip with no `gh` call, reported as `claimed`, `abandoned` or `waiting`), and only then sort least recently examined first (an issue with no `considered` time first, then the oldest `considered` time, ties by ascending issue number) and keep the first `max_candidates` (default 10, [PROPOSAL])**. A `pending`, `launched` or `unknown` entry older than the lease is NOT dropped: it stays a candidate, so a claim that stopped after the launch is retried or reported `abandoned` by step 7 (G9), and one whose claim finished is turned into `claimed` by step 6 and dropped here from the next cycle on. The claim leaves the issue in Backlog, assigned to the own login and labelled (Section 2.1), so every handled issue stays in the discovery result until a person moves it; capping before the local skip would let the lowest-numbered 10 handled issues hide every new one [DESIGN]. Bound: an entry past the lease costs at most one candidate slot before it becomes terminal (`claimed`, `abandoned`) or is retried. An issue that is examined but stays unhandled (for example labelled but not yet approved, rejected by a filter, or waiting) is stamped in `considered` at step 4, so the next cycle sorts it behind every issue not examined more recently; with more than `max_candidates` such issues the window therefore rotates through all of them, one slice of `max_candidates` per cycle, instead of re-examining the same lowest-numbered ones forever and starving a new issue [DESIGN, Gate 3 finding F1] | discovery error: exit 2 |
| 4 | For each number: `snapshot(gh, n)`, then `passes_cheap_filters` (filters 1, 5, open); `record_considered(key, now)` is called for every number examined here, before any filter decides | rejected with the filter name |
| 5 | For survivors: `label_events` then `is_approved` (filter 2) | rejected with the actor and event id in the detail; `E-GH-TRUNCATED` from `label_events` rejects the issue with reason `approval-unverifiable` (fail closed) |
| 6 | `own_claim_comment_ids(gh, n, login=own_login)`: any id means the claim already ran for this login | skip, reason `already-claimed`; this check runs even when the local record is missing (W02 recommendation 3), and when it finds a marker (and `cfg.dry_run` is false) the cycle sets the local entry to `claimed` with detail `marker-found` (a new entry with `attempts` 0 when none exists; an existing entry keeps its `attempts`), whatever state it was in, so that step 3 drops the issue before the cap in later cycles; a marker also ends any retry, because the claim ran to its last write; `E-GH-TRUNCATED` skips the issue with reason `claim-marker-unverifiable` (fail closed) |
| 7 | `may_dispatch(store entry, ...)` (entries still in the lease were already dropped by step 3, so what reaches here is new, `failed`, or `pending`, `launched` or `unknown` past the lease) | `wait`, `abandon` (reported) or proceed (`launch` or `retry-launch`) |
| 8 | Final `snapshot(gh, n)` right before the launch, the pre-launch re-read and the only re-read in the cycle (W01 §14.3 step 3): still open, still assigned to the own login only, approval label still present | skip, reason `changed-before-launch` |
| 9 | If `cfg.dry_run`: record the decision `would-launch` and continue | no record, no launch |
| 10 | If launches this cycle `>= max_launches`: skip with `cycle-cap` | |
| 11 | Write the entry (key `(repository, issue number)`, `pending`, `attempts + 1`, `launch_cwd = str(cfg.worktree)`, approver login and approval event id from step 5; an approval event id that differs from the stored one is reported as `re-approved`) with `store.save` before launching | save error: do not launch, exit 1 |
| 12 | `launch_claim(n, cfg.worktree, orca=orca, retry_operation_id=..., which=which)` where the id is set only for the `retry-launch` action | update the entry to `launched`, `unknown` (keep the id, or none after a timeout) or `failed` (only when no process was started or the refusal is explicit) |
| 13 | Emit the audit record: `issue`, `decision`, `reason`, `approver`, `approval_event_id`, `operation_id`, `attempts` (subset of the audit fields W02 J2 proposes, [PROPOSAL]); never any issue text | |

There is no sleep or in-cycle retry. A `CommandError` from a per-issue call skips that issue and is reported; a failure of steps 2 or 3 ends the cycle. The 5-minute cadence is the back-off. How to honor `retry-after` and `x-ratelimit-reset` through `gh` is not shown by the research: open Q23.

**Options of `once`** (names and defaults are [PROPOSAL] unless a source is given):

| Option | Required | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `--worktree-path PATH` | yes | absolute path of the existing Orca checkout of the fork; becomes `--worktree path:<abs>` (Q17) |
| `--trigger-label TEXT`, `--approval-label TEXT` | yes | label names; none exist yet and no file chose them (R02 §2; Q9) |
| `--approvers-file PATH` | yes | approver allow-list file (location open: Q10) |
| `--project-owner TEXT`, `--project-number INT` | yes unless `--label-only` | the Project to read (owner and number appear in no file: Q9) |
| `--label-only` | no | skip filters 3 and 4; a degraded mode that is allowed only if the owner accepts it (Q9; W01 §14.3 fallback) |
| `--state-file PATH` | no | default `~/.local/state/superclaude/issue-watch/dispatched.json` (path shape from W02 A4; whether `$HOME` persists across a workspace stop is unknown: Q15) |
| `--max-candidates INT`, `--max-launches INT`, `--max-attempts INT`, `--lease-minutes INT` | no | defaults 10, 1, 3, 60 (Q13) |
| `--dry-run` | no | read-only calls only; no record, no launch |

**Exit codes of `once`** [PROPOSAL, in the table form of `origin_repo.py:45-50`]:

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | cycle completed (including nothing to do, a held lock, dry-run, per-issue skips) |
| 1 | usage or configuration error: bad option, missing or empty approver file, wrong origin of the checkout, record could not be saved |
| 2 | a GitHub read needed for the whole cycle failed (login or discovery) |
| 3 | the cycle completed but at least one launch failed, ended `unknown`, or an issue was abandoned |

**Test scenarios** (`FakeRunner` for `gh` and `git`, `FakeOrca` for `orca`, a `tmp_path` store, an injected `now`):

| Test | Asserts |
|---|---|
| Happy path | one candidate passes all five filters; exactly one `orca agent launch` call (after the `skills list --json` probe of `resolve_orca`) with the exact argv of Phase 3; the store file already contains the `pending` entry when `FakeOrca` is called (the mock reads the file inside the call); final entry `launched` |
| Second cycle | same GitHub state and no marker, entry `launched` by the first cycle (the claim stopped after the launch, as with `E-TIER-UNAVAILABLE`): inside the lease no second `orca` call and the issue is reported `waiting`; after the lease (the injected `now` is advanced past it) exactly one `launch` (attempts 2); after `max_attempts` the entry is `abandoned`, reported, exit 3, no further launch. Variant with a marker comment present after the lease: no `orca` call, the entry becomes `claimed`, and the next cycle drops it in step 3 before the cap |
| Lost record | empty store but the marker comment id list is non-empty: no launch, and a `claimed` entry with detail `marker-found` is written |
| Changed before launch | the final snapshot shows a second assignee: no launch |
| Cycle cap | two eligible issues, `max_launches=1`: one launch, one `cycle-cap` skip |
| Dry run | gh reads happen; no `orca` call; no store file |
| Unknown outcome | entry `unknown` with the id; inside the lease the next cycle skips it (reported `waiting`, no `orca` call); once the injected `now` is advanced past the lease, the next cycle's `orca` argv contains `--retry-launch <id>`, is otherwise the original argv and runs from the stored `launch_cwd`; no launch call without it; if `--worktree-path` differs from the stored `launch_cwd`: `abandon` with `cwd-changed`, no call |
| Launch timeout | `FakeOrca` in `timeout` mode: the entry becomes `unknown` with no id (not `failed`), exit 3; the next cycle inside the lease makes no `orca agent launch` call; after the lease the entry is `abandoned` and reported, still no launch call and no call without `--retry-launch`, so no second session is ever started for the issue |
| Many already-handled candidates | more than `max_candidates` discovery results of which the lowest-numbered `max_candidates` are already handled, plus one new eligible issue. Case 1: those carry a `claimed` or `abandoned` entry, or a `launched` entry still inside the lease: all are dropped before the cap and the new issue is examined and launched in the same cycle (the cap applies after the cheap local skips, step 3 of the algorithm). Case 2: those carry only the own claim marker and no local entry: after one cycle they are recorded `claimed` with detail `marker-found`, and the next cycle drops them and examines the new issue. Case 3: one of them has a `launched` entry older than the lease and a claim marker: it is examined once (step 6 sets `claimed`, no launch) and from the next cycle on it is dropped as `claimed` in step 3 and consumes no cap slot, so it does not hide the new issue again |
| Unhandled issues cannot monopolize the cap | 15 discovery results numbered 1 to 15 that are labelled, in Backlog and assigned to the own login but not approved (so each is examined and rejected, never handled), `max_candidates` 10, plus one approved eligible issue numbered 99 (the highest number). Cycle 1 examines issues 1 to 10 (no `considered` time, ascending number), rejects them and stamps `considered`; issue 99 is not examined yet. Cycle 2 sorts the never-examined issues 11 to 15 and 99 before the stamped ones, examines them, and launches issue 99 (the fake `gh` call log shows `snapshot` for 99 in cycle 2 at the latest, and issues 1 to 10 are not examined again before 11 to 15 and 99). Cycle 3 with an advanced `now` examines the oldest-stamped issues first. The test fails if the cap is applied in ascending issue-number order only (Gate 3 finding F1) |
| Launch failure | `failed`, attempts incremented, exit 3 |
| Lock held | a second `poll_lock` held by the test: exit 0 with zero calls |
| GitHub failure | login or discovery returns exit 1: exit 2, zero `orca` calls |
| Wrong origin | `git` returns another repo: exit 1 before any `gh` call |
| Hostile text | the snapshot and events fixtures contain a hostile `title`, `body` and comment text although none was requested; no `orca` argv element and no audit line contains it; the prompt is exactly `/sc:issue --claim <int>` |
| Non-integer discovery value | `"331"`, `3.0`, `True` from discovery are dropped and listed in `dropped`; no call is made for them |
| Truncated read | the events fixture is a raw page of 100 events for every page up to `max_pages`, each page holding fewer than 100 label events (for example 40), so the filtered `items` are shorter than 100 while `n == 100`: the issue is rejected with `approval-unverifiable`, no `orca` call, no store write; the comments fixture (raw pages of 100 comments, few of them own claim comments) likewise gives `claim-marker-unverifiable`; a page with `n == 100` but fewer than 100 filtered items followed by a page with `n < 100` is read to its end and decided normally; a single raw page with `n < 100` is read to its end and decided normally |
| Registration of the verb | `["issue-watch", "--help"]` lists `once` (extends step 2.6) |

### Phase 6: End-to-end tests with PATH stubs, acceptance criteria and quality gates

**Goal:** Prove with the repo's own `gh` mocking style that the real entry point, the real `subprocess` runner and the real argv reach fake `gh`, `git` and `orca` executables, and run every gate the repo applies. Nothing live.
**Dependencies:** Phases 2 to 5.

The repo's actual way of mocking `gh` is a bash executable named `gh` written into a temp directory that is prepended to `PATH` in the child's environment, with the code under test run as a real subprocess and the stub logging its argv for the test to read back (R02 §3.4, [CODE-VERIFIED]): `tests/pr_submit/test_conflict_e2e.py:118-146,166-169` (`_GH_STUB`, `install_gh_stub`, `gh(repos, *args)` running `["gh", *args]`), `tests/pr_submit/test_conflict_scripts.py:161-200` (`make_repos`, the `bin_dir` and `PATH` prefix at l.179), `test_codex_reviews.py:554-581`, `test_static_grep.py:341-378`, `test_cosmos_poll.py:100-190` (a stub that logs argv as JSON with `jq -nc --args '$ARGS.positional' -- "$@"` and rejects calls lacking `--repo` with exit 91). No Python-level `gh` mock exists to reuse; `mock_gh` patches a payload function (R02 §3.1). **The new tests use both styles:** the injected runner for unit and cycle tests (Phases 2 to 5) and the PATH stub for the end-to-end tests below, so the argv that actually reaches an executable is asserted.

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | Stub installer | `tests/issue_watch/conftest.py` (extend) | `def install_stubs(tmp_path: Path, *, gh_script: str, git_script: str, orca_script: str) -> Path`: writes executables `gh`, `git`, `orca` and also `orca-ide` (the other candidate name of `resolve_orca`; the same script, so every name the lookup tries resolves to a stub) into `tmp_path / "bin"`, `chmod(0o755)`, returns the directory; a fixture sets `PATH` to that directory only (`monkeypatch.setenv("PATH", str(bin_dir))`, not a prefix of the real `PATH`, so no real `orca`, `orca-ide`, `gh` or `git` can be found, which keeps the "nothing runs live" rule; the stubs call only shell builtins and `jq`, and the stub script uses an absolute `#!/bin/bash` and the absolute path of `jq` resolved before `PATH` is replaced) [DESIGN]. The prefix form `f"{bin_dir}{os.pathsep}{os.environ['PATH']}"` of `make_repos` (`test_conflict_scripts.py:161-180`) is not copied, because on this host the real `orca-ide` is on the real `PATH`. Each stub logs its argv to a calls file as one JSON line (the `test_cosmos_poll.py:100-190` technique, which needs `jq`; skip the e2e tests with `pytest.skip` when `shutil.which("jq")` is `None`), serves canned JSON from `tests/issue_watch/fixtures/` (for a `gh api ... --jq <filter>` call the stub pipes the raw fixture page through `jq -c "<filter>"` with the filter it received, so the real `events-jq`/`marker-jq` are exercised and `{n, items}` is produced from raw pages [DESIGN]), and exits 99 with `unexpected call` for any argv it does not recognise (the `orca` stub recognises the `resolve_orca` probe `skills list --json` and answers `{"topics":[{"name":"orca-cli"}]}`). The `gh` stub exits 91 when a call lacks `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` and is not `api user`, `project item-list --owner ...` or a `repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/...` path (the `test_cosmos_poll.py` rule) |
| 6.2 | End-to-end tests | `tests/issue_watch/test_e2e_pathstub.py` (NEW) | `CliRunner().invoke(main, ["issue-watch", "once", "--worktree-path", str(tmp_path / "checkout"), ...])` with `PATH` restricted to the stub directory (the invocation style of `tests/recommend/test_cli_registration.py` and `tests/cli/test_init_lite.py:34-35`, R02 §5; the verb is run with the default `which=None`, which resolves to the real `shutil.which` at call time and now finds only the stubs: `orca-ide` is tried first and its stub answers the probe, so no real binary is probed or launched). Asserts: exit 0; each stub logs the program name it was started as (`$0` basename) next to its argv, and the probe and the launch are logged under `orca-ide`, the first candidate name, so the test shows that the run went through a stub and not through a real binary; the `orca` calls file has exactly one `agent launch` line (plus the `skills list --json` probe line of `resolve_orca`, which the `orca` stub answers with `{"topics":[{"name":"orca-cli"}]}`) and that line's argv equals the list in the Phase 3 contract table; the `gh` calls file contains no write-shaped call (token check in the style of `assert_no_retrigger_call`, `test_conflict_e2e.py:178-186`, whole tokens: `edit`, `comment`, `create`, `-X`, `--add-assignee`, and a `--method` other than `GET`); no recorded argv contains `SuperClaude-Org`; the audit lines on stdout contain the issue number and approver and no issue text; a second invocation with the same stub state produces no second `orca` line |
| 6.3 | Static guard tests | `tests/issue_watch/test_static_guards.py` (NEW) | Reads the package source files and asserts: no `shell=True`, no `os.system`, no `os.popen` (the poller is a plain argv-list caller); no string `SuperClaude-Org`; the only occurrence of the literal `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` as a repo constant is in `models.py` (`REPO`). The repo already asserts repo-scoping of `gh` lines in bash scripts with regex helpers (`test_static_grep.py:30,:106`, R02 §3.4); here the argv are Python lists, so the dynamic checks of steps 4.5 and 6.2 carry that rule and this test adds only the no-shell checks |
| 6.4 | Acceptance criteria (copied into the design doc, step 1.10) | `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` | 1. `superclaude issue-watch --help` lists `once`. 2. The only text derived from an issue in any launch argument is one integer from 1 to 999999999, and the prompt equals `/sc:issue --claim <int>`. 3. No `gh` call made by the package is write-shaped, and every repository-scoped call pins `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` (the only exempt calls are `gh api user` and `gh project item-list --owner`). 4. An issue is selected only if it passes all five filters (tagged, human-approved by an allow-listed actor, in the Project, Status Backlog, assigned to the own login only), or `--label-only` is given and logged. 5. The dispatch record exists before the launch is called. 6. A second cycle on unchanged state starts no second session. 7. A lost record with an own claim marker on the issue starts no session. 8. `--dry-run` makes no launch and writes no record. 9. An unknown launch outcome is retried only with `--retry-launch <operationId>`. 10. `uv run pytest tests/issue_watch/ -v` passes with `gh`, `git` and `orca` mocked and nothing run live; ruff, `make lint-architecture` and `make verify-deps` pass |
| 6.5 | Run the gates | none (commands) | `uv run pytest tests/issue_watch/ -v`; `uv run pytest tests/cli/test_cli_registration.py tests/issue_watch/test_cli_registration.py -v`; `make format` then `uv run ruff format --check src/ tests/`; `uv run ruff check src/ tests/`; **`make sync-dev` first, then** `uv run pytest` (CI runs `make sync-dev` before pytest because "several audit tests assert byte-identical parity between src/superclaude/... and the .claude/ mirrors" and the mirrors are gitignored, `.github/workflows/test.yml:32-38`; then `pytest -v --tb=short --color=yes -n auto --maxfail=3 --ignore=tests/cli/test_verify_sync_hooks.py`, `test.yml:46-47`; in this worktree `.claude/skills` and `.claude/commands` do not exist until `make sync-dev` creates them, checked 2026-11-07), and `make verify-sync` after it (Makefile targets `sync-dev` l.109 and `verify-sync` l.166); the mirror output under `.claude/` is gitignored and is never staged; `make lint-architecture`; `make verify-deps`. Smoke check only: `uv run superclaude issue-watch --help` and `uv run superclaude issue-watch once --help` (no GitHub call). Only declared pytest markers may be used (`--strict-markers`, `pyproject.toml:107-117`); tests write only under `tmp_path`, never under `docs/mistakes` or `docs/memory/solutions_learned.jsonl` (`tests/conftest.py` pollution guard, R02 §5) |

### Phase 7: Trigger and approval label setup instructions, approver and scope setup, operator notes

**Goal:** Instructions a maintainer can follow later; nothing in this phase is run. They go into the design doc as a section, so no new file type or index is needed.
**Dependencies:** Phases 1 and 5 (option names).

| Step | Action | Files | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | Label creation lines in the house format | `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md` (section "Label and approver setup") | One-time maintainer lines in the form of `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/refs/labels.md:22-42`: `gh label create "<trigger-label>" --repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` and `gh label create "<approval-label>" --repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`. The names are placeholders because none exists and no file chose them (the fork has only the 9 default labels: R02 §2): open Q9. State who may do what: creating a label needs Write, applying a label needs Triage or higher, 3 accounts hold such a role today (R03 §4.2, observed 2026-11-07). State that `/sc:issue` never creates labels and `--claim` changes none (`refs/labels.md:7`), so these lines are the only way the labels come to exist. Also state that the `type: *` and `complexity: *` labels `/sc:issue --create` expects are missing on the fork and are not part of this work (R02 §2b; owner choice) |
| 7.2 | Approver allow-list setup | same section | The file read by `--approvers-file`: one GitHub login per line, `#` comments, to be filled with the Triage-or-higher accounts that may approve. Where the file lives (repo file, org team, CODEOWNERS-like list) is open: Q10. Explain the rule the poller applies (latest `labeled` event of the approval label by an allow-listed actor) and its limits: an edit of the issue body after approval is not detectable (Q11), and the claim reads title, body and comments live (Q12) |
| 7.3 | Per-developer Project read scope | same section | Each polling `gh` login needs `read:project` to read the org Project; the observed workspace token lacks it and `gh project list --owner IronbellyOrg` failed with "missing required scopes [read:project]" (R03 §4.2). Documented command form: `gh auth refresh -s read:project` (external context https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_auth_refresh; acceptance of this exact scope name and the `GH_TOKEN`-injected case are [UNVERIFIED]: Q7). A fine-grained token with organization Projects = Read is the least-privilege alternative (W01 §9, external context). `--label-only` avoids the scope at the cost of filters 3 and 4 (Q9) |
| 7.4 | Routing and Project set-up | same section | Intended-owner rule as implemented (routing candidate 1): a Triage-or-higher person assigns the issue to the developer, and the issue must be in the Project with Status Backlog; the Project owner, number and Status name are open: Q9, Q4. State the consequence the research records: the assignee persists after the claim, so it cannot mark "already dispatched" (R01 §9) |
| 7.5 | Scheduler follow-up text (Coder repo, not built here) | same section, "Follow-up" | The in-workspace scheduling primitive that the research documents (external context https://registry.terraform.io/providers/coder/coder/latest/docs/resources/script): a `coder_script` with `cron = "0 */5 * * * *"` (six fields, seconds first; the provider's own example is a five-minute job) whose `script` runs the verb. Facts to state: the cron job runs only while the workspace runs and is not Coder "activity", so it neither keeps a workspace alive nor polls while stopped (W02 A2, A3); issues approved meanwhile wait on GitHub until the next start; the verb takes its own lock because overlap behavior of a still-running cron script is [UNVERIFIED] (Q15); whether `$HOME`, hence the default state file, survives a stop is unknown (Q15). Example command line shown as text only: `superclaude issue-watch once --worktree-path <checkout> --trigger-label <name> --approval-label <name> --approvers-file <path> --project-owner <org> --project-number <n>` |
| 7.6 | Owner-run live test checklist (written, not run) | same section | Each check is a single read-only or single-launch action by the owner, with the question it closes: (1) launch with a harmless leading slash command as `--prompt` and see whether it runs, and whether argv or typed paste (Q1, Q2); (2) confirm the documented retry rule on the target host (`orca agent launch --help`: re-issue the same command from the same directory with `--retry-launch <operationId>`) (Q3); (3) `gh project item-list <n> --owner <org> --query "status:Backlog label:<name> assignee:@me" --format json -L 100` accepts the filter and show its JSON shape (Q8); (4) `gh auth refresh -s read:project` works on a workspace login (Q7); (5) one workspace's permission mode for a launched session (Q6); (6) scheduler availability, overlap and `$HOME` persistence in the target template (Q15); (7) the REST comment object's author field name (Q11); (8) whether the `--worktree` path used is the right checkout (Q17); (9) the per-host preflight checklist of step 1.10 on one workspace: Orca runtime reachable by a scheduler-started process, `ccsession-orca` configured, `claude-gw-tier3/2/1a` picker IDs served, `gh auth status` as the person, ClickUp variables (Q5); (10) measure the duration of one claim to set the lease (Q13); (11) run one claim twice on the same issue to see whether an existing `.dev/issues/<N>/` directory is tolerated (Q18) |

### Test plan summary

| Test file (all NEW, under `tests/issue_watch/`) | Style | Pins |
|---|---|---|
| `test_cli_registration.py` | CliRunner against `main` | group in `--help`, verb `once` listed |
| `test_runner.py` | monkeypatched `subprocess.run` | argv list, no shell, missing tool and timeout mapped to `CommandError` |
| `test_prompt_validation.py` | parametrized pure function | integer-only prompt (owner decision D3) |
| `test_launch_contract.py` | `FakeOrca` | exact `orca agent launch` argv, outcomes `started`, `failed`, `unknown`, `--retry-launch`, origin check |
| `test_selection.py` | pure functions over JSON fixtures | each of the five filters, approver rule, shared "assigned to someone else" predicate |
| `test_github_reads.py` | `FakeRunner` | read-only calls, repo pin, `--method GET`, no title, body or comments requested, explicit limit |
| `test_state.py` | `tmp_path` | atomic save, corrupt file, lock contention, `may_dispatch` table |
| `test_cycle.py` | `FakeRunner` plus `FakeOrca` | record-before-launch, no relaunch, lost record, caps, dry run, hostile text, failures |
| `test_cli_once.py` | CliRunner with `build_runners` and `shutil.which` monkeypatched (a fake lookup, never the real `PATH`; the patch takes effect because `which` defaults to `None` and is resolved to `shutil.which` at call time, step 3.4) | options, exit codes, audit lines |
| `test_e2e_pathstub.py` | PATH-stub executables for `gh`, `git`, `orca` | real subprocess path and recorded argv |
| `test_static_guards.py` | source scan | no shell, no upstream repo string |

Commands: see step 6.5. Nothing in the test plan calls GitHub, Orca or Coder for real.

### Rollback and disable considerations

| Situation | Action | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The code is merged but nothing schedules it | Nothing runs; removing the two-line registration block in `src/superclaude/cli/main.py` and the package directory reverts it with no effect on other commands | R02 §4.1: registrations are independent blocks; no `pyproject.toml` change |
| A scheduler entry exists (follow-up in the Coder repo) | Disable by removing or disabling the `coder_script` cron entry in the template; the verb has no daemon mode, so nothing keeps running after the entry is gone | W02 A1 (external context) |
| Stop selection without touching the scheduler | Remove the approval label from the issue, unassign the developer, or empty the approver file (an empty or missing file makes the cycle exit 1 and approve nothing, step 4.3) | R03 §4.3 |
| Inspect without acting | `superclaude issue-watch once --dry-run` makes read-only calls, no launch and no record | step 5.3 |
| Bound the damage of a bad cycle | `max_launches` (default 1) and `max_attempts` (default 3) cap sessions per cycle and per issue [PROPOSAL values: Q13] | W02 recommendation 7 |
| Reset the local record | Delete the state file; an issue that already carries the own claim marker is still not relaunched because the marker check runs first | W02 recommendation 3 |
| A session was already launched | Nothing in this design recalls or closes it: a started Orca session stays open after the claim stops [UNVERIFIED inference, Q21]; the person closes it | R01 §12, §Gaps |

### Follow-up, not in this plan

The owner decided option A. These items are recorded, not planned. Nothing here is built, scheduled or deployed by this plan.

| Item | Why it is not in this plan | Boundary or source |
|---|---|---|
| Option B: GitHub Action selects and calls an authenticated workspace listener | Not the owner's choice; needs the Coder#477 listener, registration and transport plus the first `schedule:`/`issues:` workflow in this repo (none exists: R02 §1) and a privileged token | The listener, registration and transport are Coder repo work (IronbellyOrg/Coder#477); this repo would own only the selector/sender side and the contract (S03 §6.2; W02 J1, J2) |
| Option C: central microservice (GitHub App, webhooks) plus the same listener | Not the owner's choice; needs a service to host (hosting not established), a GitHub App and the Coder#477 listener | Same boundary (S03 §6.3; W01 §12) |
| IronbellyOrg/Coder#477 | Filed assuming a push design; option A makes it unnecessary unless B or C is revisited. Whether to relabel, narrow or close it is a decision for its owner in the Coder repo | NOTES ADDENDUM 2 |
| Option D: label relay (a scheduled Action writes a "ready" marker, workspaces poll it label-only) | Adds a workflow, a secret and a label while keeping the poller; first fallback if workspace tokens cannot read the org Project (revisit condition 6) | S03 §6.4 |
| Scheduler entry and persisted state in the Coder template | Lives in the Coder repo (`coder_script` cron, `$HOME` persistence); only the instruction is written here (step 7.5) | W02 A1, A4; GAPS 9 |
| Headless launch variant `claude -p "/sc:issue --claim <int>"` | Changes only the launch step; the owner chose the Orca route; whether the claim completes under `-p` is untested | W02 C1 to C3; GAPS 8 |
| A "wake a stopped workspace" design | Mentioned for push designs only (`coder ssh` auto-start); no research file evaluates it as its own design | S03 §6.4 "Further hybrids" |
| Live deployment and the owner-run live test | Out of scope by the owner decision; the checklist is written in step 7.6 | NOTES ADDENDUM 2 |
| A claim-side change to how `/sc:issue --claim` reads untrusted issue text | Not part of a claim-only watcher; the policy is open (Q12) | R03 §3.2 |
| `/sc:issue --claim --pipeline <level>` and the launch-level guard | Design only; B-CTX gates only that later extension | R03 §1.2, §1.3 |

**Revisit conditions for options B and C** (the owner decided A; revisit only if one of these is shown to hold; none has been observed; from S03 "Revisit conditions"):

| # | Condition | Option it would favor | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Workspaces are mostly stopped and approved issues wait too long; the owner wants a design that can start a stopped workspace | B or C (wake through the Coder control plane at the price of a privileged token; a push design does not recover missed webhooks by itself) | W02 J3 item 1, B2; W01 §12, §14.5 |
| 2 | Poll latency or API use becomes a problem (many workspaces share one login, a very large unfiltered Project, GraphQL-only polling) | B or C | W02 J3 item 2; W01 §14.2 |
| 3 | A need for central audit and one approver policy that per-workspace pollers cannot enforce consistently | B or C | W02 J3 item 3; R03 §7 |
| 4 | Double claims by the same login in several workspaces, or by two logins, occur despite the best-effort checks | B or C (single decision point plus durable idempotency key) | W02 J3 item 4, G1, G7; R01 §9 (window between two different logins estimated at seconds to minutes, collision possible but not frequent, an estimate from reading the code, not measured; the same-login window is longer, to the Wave 6 comment, with no estimate) |
| 5 | Coder ships the workspace-to-account binding as a platform feature | B or C | W02 J3 item 5, E1 |
| 6 | The workspace `gh` token cannot read the org Project while a central credential can | D first; B or C if a GitHub App with org Projects read is preferred | W01 §14.5, §9; R03 §4.2 |
| 7 | A need to react to a Project Status change at event time or to see who moved an issue to Backlog | C (`projects_v2_item` webhook with `changes`) | W01 §3, §14.5 |
| 8 | A need for a shared at-most-once ledger across logins or workspaces | B or C | W01 §14.5 |
| 9 | Workspaces cannot run a recurring poller (the cron primitive is unusable in the target template or state cannot persist) | Not settled by the research; a central selector would be considered | W02 A1, A4, A5; GAPS 9 |

If none holds, option A stays and Coder#477 is unnecessary (S03; NOTES ADDENDUM 2).

- [ ] `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/` contains `__init__.py`, `commands.py`, `runner.py`, `models.py`, `github.py`, `selection.py`, `launch.py`, `state.py`, `cycle.py`
- [ ] `src/superclaude/cli/main.py` has exactly one new block after the `reflect` registration (l.559-561) with the `# noqa: E402,I001` marker and `main.add_command(issue_watch_group, name="issue-watch")`
- [ ] No `pyproject.toml` change, no new dependency, no `.claude/` mirror change, no workflow change

- [ ] The only issue-derived launch text is one integer built by `build_claim_prompt`; no `--json` list requests title, body or comments, and the Project and `api` reads are narrowed with `--jq` so that no issue text reaches the poller (SC-5)
- [ ] All five filters (tagged, human-approved, in the Project, Status Backlog, intended owner) are implemented and tested; `--label-only` is logged as degraded
